# 테스트 매트릭스

test-author가 소유한다. Phase 2에서 SPEC의 모든 수용 기준을 행으로 옮기고, red-team 시나리오를 병합한다.

## 형식

| ID | SPEC | 계층 | 우선순위 | Given | When | Then | 테스트 파일 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

- ID: `T-<SPEC번호>-<일련>` (예: T-06-03). red-team 출신은 `T-RT-<일련>`. 전역 설정(5절)은 `T-CFG-*`, 비기능 보안(6절)·교차 보안은 `T-SEC-*`, 브라우저 E2E는 `T-E2E-*`, 소유자가 직접 제시한 운영 시나리오(S-1..S-6) 출신은 `T-OS-<일련>` (note 32). Screen contracts that the implementation defined rather than SPEC (no acceptance criterion behind them) are `T-UI-<serial>` (note 40).
- 계층: `unit`(Jenkins 불필요) / `integration`(JenkinsRule) / `e2e`(브라우저·REST, Phase 5)
- 우선순위: P0(릴리스 차단) / P1(릴리스 전 수정) / P2(다음 릴리스)
- 차단 단언 공통 기준: 큐 비어 있음 + `getNextBuildNumber()` 불변 + `waitUntilNoActivity()` 후 빌드 없음.

| T-SEC-07 | 6절 (P-03 decided: **keep the current behaviour**, option ①; note 44) | integration | P0 | Run control on, approval-required job with **two** parameters: a Password parameter `SECRET_TOKEN` whose definition default is a second distinctive plaintext, and a non-sensitive `PLAIN_PARAM` whose default differs from the value this row types. Requester u1, designated approver a1, a ViewHistory holder | u1 fills in the per-job request form as a browser does — a plaintext typed into the concealed password control, a value typed into the plain control — submits it; a1 approves; the build runs | **No plaintext anywhere**: not in the stored request record, not on the request detail screen (seen by requester, approver and administrator), not in any file under `$JENKINS_HOME/batch-control/`, not in `requests.csv`/`runs.csv`/`changes.csv`/`incidents.csv`, not on the dashboard. Masked, not dropped: the secret parameter is still recorded **by name** and its value carries the documented `********` mask (ARCHITECTURE section 5). The **accepted limitation** of P-03 is pinned in the same row: the approved build receives the mask, so the original secret is provably not reproduced on execution. **False-positive guard (the core of this row)**: on every one of those surfaces `PLAIN_PARAM` must be present with exactly the value typed — stored verbatim, rendered, exported and passed to the build — so an implementation that stores, renders or exports nothing cannot pass | SecretParameterMaskingTest |
| T-SEC-19 | 6절 (P-03, note 44) | integration | P0 | Run control on; a job outside run control carrying the same two parameter definitions, activated first (SPEC 6a / D-46: the direct build below is a cause-less, unattended submission and needs activation regardless of approvalRequired, even on an otherwise uncontrolled job; note 109) | the job is built directly with a real `ParametersAction(PasswordParameterValue, StringParameterValue)` — the path every run that does **not** go through an approval takes (cron, upstream, an uncontrolled job) | the run record leaks no plaintext: none in any file under `$JENKINS_HOME/batch-control/`, none in `runs.csv`, none on the dashboard, while the secret parameter is still named there; `PLAIN_PARAM`'s value is present on both surfaces (same false-positive guard). The guarantee must not depend on the request screen — this is the ARCHITECTURE section 5 store rule ("`PasswordParameterValue` and `Secret` are stored as `********`") rather than the action-layer masking of T-SEC-07 | SecretParameterMaskingTest |

| T-SEC-27 | 12 (S-22, D-18 extension) | unit | P0 | The CSV cell encoder | `"\t=1+1"`, `"\r=1+1"`, `"\n=1+1"`, `" =1+1"`, `"  \t -2+3"`, `"\t@SUM(1,2)"` and `"\t+cmd|'/C calc'!A0"` are encoded | every cell's value starts with `'` and is otherwise the original bytes (leading whitespace preserved - these are audit exports). Separately: `"\t=1+1"` carries no CR, LF, comma or quote, so it must come out prefixed and **unquoted** - the prefix and RFC 4180 quoting are independent and a row that conflates them passes for the wrong reason | ui/CsvFormulaPrefixTest |
| T-SEC-28 | 12 (S-22) | unit | P0 | The CSV cell encoder | `"\r=1+1"` (and `"\n=1+1"`, `"\t@SUM(1,2)"`) are encoded | the cell opens with `"` and the first character **inside** the quotes is `'`; the whole cell is the quoted, prefixed original. A prefix placed outside the quotes fails this | ui/CsvFormulaPrefixTest |
| T-SEC-29 | 12 (S-22, falsifiability twin) | unit | P0 | The CSV cell encoder | `"x"`, `""`, `"   "` (all whitespace), `"\t\thello"` and `"a=1+1"` are encoded | each comes back unchanged with no `'` added - an all-whitespace cell gaining a prefix is a failure. Guard in the same row: `"=1+1"` and `" =1+1"` are still neutralised, so these negatives cannot pass against an encoder that prefixes nothing | ui/CsvFormulaPrefixTest |

| T-SEC-31 | 12 (S-22, vector found during the fix) | integration | P0 | Run control on; a job **named** `" =1+1"` (leading space) which is built once and carries one run request | a ViewHistory holder GETs `history/requests.csv`, `history/runs.csv` and `history/changes.csv` | first the premise: such a job **can** be created - core's name check rejects ISO control characters and its own unsafe set, neither of which covers a leading space (if this ever starts failing, core closed the vector and the row needs re-deciding). | T-SEC-33 | 9 (security-07 S-02, #20) | integration | P1 | Change control on; a FreeStyle job whose `description` contains the text `cmd plugin="custom-thing@1.0" tail` (the same `name@version` shape T-09-13 uses for a genuine attribute) | POST config.xml changing only the version digit inside that embedded substring (`1.0`→`2.0`) | exactly one new CONFIGURE record with a diff containing `2.0` — the plugin="name@version" noise stripper (T-09-13) may not match text that is not a real `plugin` attribute. | T-UI-100 | 6 (D-60), D-66 | integration | P1 | approval-required `param-x` with string P (default `p-default`); p0 without Batch Control permissions | u1 GETs `job/param-x/batch-control/` and `?p.P=routed-100`; p0 GETs the page | form posting to `<job>/batch-control/submit` with P `p-default`, then `routed-100`. Guard: p0 404 (note 247) | RequestDialogTest |
| T-UI-101 | 8, D-66 | integration | P2 | change control on; g1 | g1 GETs `batch-control/grants/?scopeFullName=batch-x` (redirects not followed) and `batch-control/grants/` | the former link redirects (3xx) to `grants/new?scopeFullName=batch-x`. Guard: the bare grants page answers 200 (note 249) | RequestDialogTest |
| T-UI-102 | 8, D-66 | integration | P2 | 120 rejected grant requests and one pending | admin GETs `grants/?endedPage=1..k`, then `grants/?pendingPage=2` | page 1 of `table[data-batch-control-list=ended]` shows fewer than 120; the ended pages together reach all 120; the pending row is in `table[data-batch-control-list=pending]` on every ended page; `pendingPage=2` leaves the ended list as on page 1 (note 249) | RequestLayoutTest |
| T-UI-103 | 6a, D-66 | integration | P2 | u1's pending ACTIVATE request designating a1; a2 an approver not designated | a1, admin, a2 GET `activations/` | a1's pending row carries `small[data-batch-control-awaiting]`; admin's row does not; a2 has no marked row (note 249) | RequestLayoutTest |
| T-UI-104 | 2 (#31), 5, 8, D-66 | integration | P1 | as T-UI-98; p0 without Batch Control permissions | u1 GETs `job/batch-x/batch-control/dialog`, p0 the same; g1 and n1 GET `grants/dialog` | u1 200 with the form posting to `batch-control/submit`; p0 404; g1 200 with the form posting to `grants/create`; n1 refused or without that form (note 249) | RequestDialogTest |

## 비고 (전제와 요청 사항)

1. **Clock 주입 API 필요.** T-07-01/03/04, T-08-03/07/12, T-12-02는 시간 경과를 실제 대기 없이 검증해야 한다(`Thread.sleep` 금지). SPEC의 "타이머 의존 없음, 검사 시점 시각 비교" 문장을 근거로, 플러그인이 `java.time.Clock`을 주입받고 테스트에서 고정/이동 가능한 교체 진입점을 제공한다고 전제한다. → 요청: core-dev에 테스트용 Clock 교체 API.
2. **PeriodicWork 수동 트리거.** 만료 판정(ExpiryPeriodicWork)·보관 정리(RetentionPeriodicWork)는 테스트에서 `doRun()` 상당을 직접 호출할 수 있다고 전제한다(1분/1일 주기 대기 불가).
3. **재시작 + 시간 경과 조합**(T-07-04, T-08-07)은 JenkinsSessionRule 두 세션 사이에서 Clock을 미래로 설정할 수 있어야 성립한다(1번 전제의 연장).
4. **cron 발화**(T-06-06/07)는 실제 crontab 대기 대신 TimerTrigger Cause를 실은 스케줄 호출로 발화를 재현한다.
5. **테스트 의존성 필요** (pom.xml은 release-manager 소유): `org.jenkins-ci.plugins:pipeline-build-step` (T-06-05/10/11/12, PoC 보고서에서 기요청), `job-dsl` (T-09-06), `role-strategy` (T-08-08), `cloudbees-folder` (T-08-11, T-09-08), `matrix-auth` (권한 시나리오 전반), `workflow-multibranch` 또는 `branch-api` (T-10-07).
6. **T-06-12 부수 효과**: build 스텝 차단 시 상위 잡이 FAILURE로 끝나는 것은 PoC에서 확인된 Jenkins 동작이며, 이 행은 그 동작을 회귀 고정한다(문서화 대상).
7. **성능 요구**(6절: 하루 5,000 실행 규모에서 최근 7일 조회 2초 이내)는 자동 회귀 매트릭스에서 제외하고 Phase 5에서 로컬 수동 측정으로 확인한다.
8. **T-RT-\* 확정 (R-1~R-8 SPEC 채택 완료).** `T-RT-<nn>`의 `<nn>`은 `docs/reports/red-team-01.md`의 RT 번호와 일치시켰다(추적성). 번호가 비는 곳(04, 08, 09, 12)은 아래 "red-team 시나리오 제외 사유" 절에 있다. 사람이 red-team 제안 R-1~R-8을 전부 SPEC에 채택했다(D-16~D-23으로 SPEC v1.0 수용 기준에 반영됨). 따라서 **모든 T-RT 행의 Then은 이제 현행 SPEC 수용 기준에서 직접 도출된 확정 행이다.** 행 안에 남아 있는 `(SPEC 보강 필요: R-n)` 표기는 채택 이전의 이력 주석이며, 해당 R-n이 D-16~D-23으로 SPEC에 반영되었다는 근거 표시로 읽는다(행 문언은 추적성을 위해 유지). R-n 없는 T-RT 행(T-RT-06, T-RT-07)은 원래부터 현행 SPEC 문언(8절 "만료 시각 경과 후 첫 권한 검사부터 거부", 3절 결재자 변경 이력)에서 직접 도출했다.
9. **ID 규칙 보충**: 전역 설정 기본값(SPEC 5절 표)은 항목 5(실행 요청)와 번호가 겹치지 않도록 `T-CFG-*`, 비기능 보안(6절)은 `T-SEC-*`를 쓴다. 기존 시드의 T-SEC-01~04는 SPEC 열이 관련 항목 번호를 가리키던 것을 그대로 유지했다. T-SEC-04는 RT-12의 실질 엣지(제어문자·초장문 이름)를 흡수해 확장했다(행 추가 없음).
10. **동시성 행 재현 방법**(T-RT-14/15/16): 2-스레드 동시 POST(ExecutorService + CyclicBarrier 상당)로 경합을 재현하고, 결과 단언은 상태·빌드 수로만 한다. T-RT-16은 Clock 이동 + PeriodicWork 수동 트리거(비고 1·2 전제)와 조합한다.
11. **T-RT-17 전제**: "scheduleBuild2 완료·onStarted 미도달" 크래시 타이밍을 재현하려면 큐 투입 직후 세션을 종료할 수 있는 훅(예: QuietDown 상태에서 투입 후 세션 재시작)이 필요하다. 재현이 불가능하면 최소한 T-04-02에 이 타이밍 명시를 추가한 통합 테스트로 근사한다. → 요청: core-dev에 복구 로직의 idempotency 키 설계 공유.
12. **RT-08 문서화 요청**: 결재자 계정은 실인(實人) 1:1, 공용·부계정 금지를 알려진 한계로 README에 명시. → 요청: `README.md` (release-manager 소유) 알려진 한계 절 추가.
13. **T-RT-16 근사 (S2)**: 테스트에서 호출 가능한 공개 "재투입" 경로가 없어(투입은 approve() 내부에서 일어남), 경합을 "만료 시각이 이미 지난 PENDING 요청에 대한 approve()(=공개 투입 경로) vs ExpiryPeriodicWork.doRun()" 2-스레드 barrier 경합으로 재현했다. 단언은 D-20의 불변식 그대로: 만료 요청은 절대 투입되지 않고(빌드 0, executedRunId=null) 최종 상태는 EXPIRED 하나로 수렴.
14. **T-RT-17 근사 채택 (S2, 비고 11 이행)**: QuietDown 상태에서 approve로 큐 투입(scheduleBuild2 완료) 후 빌드 시작 전에 JenkinsSessionRule 세션을 종료하는 방식으로 "scheduleBuild2 완료·onStarted 미도달" 갭을 재현했다. 재시작 후 복원 큐 항목 + 복구 재투입의 중복 제거를 "요청당 빌드 정확히 1건"으로 단언.
15. **T-RT-02 '재사용 차단 기록' 단언 — D-30으로 해소됨 (원 유보: S2)**: 마커 재사용 차단 자체(동일 잡 재큐·타 잡 재투입 모두 불가, 빌드 수 불변)는 T-RT-02가 단언했으나, "차단 사실이 기록된다"의 기록 위치(로그인지 ChangeRecord인지)가 SPEC에 미정의라 단언을 유보해 두었던 항목이다. **소유자가 D-30으로 확정했다**: 재사용 시도는 감사 이력에 별도 레코드로 남고 대시보드·CSV에서 조회된다(SPEC 6절 수용 기준으로 등재, P-04는 후보 (a)로 종결). 따라서 유보는 끝났고 단언은 신규 행 **T-06-17**(레코드 + 대시보드·CSV 조회), **T-06-18**(정상 실행 1회는 레코드를 만들지 않는다 — 거짓 양성 방지), **T-06-19**(ViewHistory 없는 사용자에게 비노출)로 추가했다(`MarkerReuseAuditTest`). T-RT-02의 Then은 차단만 커버한다는 각주를 달아 두었고 행 자체는 변경하지 않았다. 남은 미정의 지점은 note 37.
16. **APPROVED-미투입 상태 만들기 (S2 전제)**: T-07-03/07-04/04-02/RT-03(approved 변형)은 `Jenkins.doQuietDown()`으로 빌드 시작을 막은 채 approve하고 필요 시 `Queue.clear()`로 큐 항목을 제거해 "APPROVED이나 큐 미투입" 상태를 만든다. 전제: approve()는 스케줄 결과와 무관하게 APPROVED 상태를 기록하며, 큐 투입 실패/유실 시에도 예외로 상태를 되돌리지 않는다.

17. **AdministrativeMonitor rows covered via activation checks (S3)**: T-08-06 and T-08-08 are asserted through `AdministrativeMonitor.isActivated()` (plus enabled/registered), not by scraping /manage HTML. Visual display of the warnings is deferred to the Phase 5 e2e pass (same convention as the T-02-02 note). T-08-06 additionally asserts the negative states (admin-only delegate, and change control off) so the warning cannot fire spuriously.
18. **Grant creation endpoint assumed (S3)**: the fixed S3 endpoint contract defines `batch-control/grants/` GET list, POST `grants/<id>/approve|reject|cancel` and POST `grants/active/<grantId>/revoke`, but names no creation endpoint. T-08-13 (creation POST without RequestGrant → 403) assumes POST `batch-control/grants/create`. → Request: ui-dev to expose grant request creation at `batch-control/grants/create` (or report the actual path so the test is adjusted).
19. **T-RT-05 assertion scope (D-17 — widened by D-31, see note 38; the cron half is SUPERSEDED by D-34, see note 46) and T-RT-20 bounded version (S3)**: T-RT-05 asserts exactly what SPEC 8/D-17 promises — a job created inside an active grant window automatically carries `approvalRequired=true`, so a MANUAL run is blocked after (and independent of) window expiry; timer runs still follow the SPEC 6 timer policy (default pass), so no cron block is asserted. T-RT-20 (P2) is implemented as a bounded regression: 30 rapid `config.xml` POSTs under an active CONFIGURE grant must leave ≥30 CONFIGURE records whose diffs chain against the previous baseline (rev-(n-1) → rev-n), plus one interleaved record of another job (pipeline not blocked). The "hundreds of saves" load variant stays a Phase 5 manual measurement (note 7).
 **Superseded (D-34):** the sentence "timer runs still follow the SPEC 6 timer policy (default pass), so no cron block is asserted" no longer holds. D-34 makes a new job start `blockTimer=true`/`blockUpstream=true`, and T-RT-05 now asserts the cron block after window expiry; the reasoning is in note 46.
20. **T-RT-02 wording aligned to D-23 (S3, spec-review-S2 carry-over)**: the row previously said the marker binds to "requestId+job+parameters"; SPEC/D-23 bind the marker to requestId (and thereby the request's job) only — no user-facing path attaches arbitrary parameters to a marker. The row's When/Then were rewritten to requestId+jobFullName binding. The existing RequestIntegrityTest assertions (re-queue and cross-job re-submission both blocked) already match this binding; no test change was needed.
21. **Grant restart rows split into GrantRestartTest (S3)**: T-08-04/07 need JenkinsSessionRule, which cannot share a class with the plain JenkinsRule used by GrantServiceTest — same split as RestartRecoveryTest in S2.
22. **T-10-07 approximated (S4)**: the multibranch project is a real `WorkflowMultiBranchProject`, but its branch comes from `SingleSCMSource` over `NullSCM` (no git fixture is available among the test dependencies, so a real SCM indexing run would be brittle). Branch builds may end FAILURE (NullSCM checks out no Jenkinsfile); the row asserts that a completion of any result is recorded for `mb/main` and that, with run control on, a manual user-cause run of the branch child is not blocked. The control-exemption is asserted only in the child's natural state (no approval property is injected — a computed child cannot be configured that way through any product path). If `SingleSCMSource` discovery behaves differently in the resolved scm-api version, re-approximate with the matrix owner.
23. **T-12-04 summary contract assumption (S4)**: the fixed S4 endpoint contract says `batch-control/history/summary?month=YYYY-MM` "returns counts" without naming a format. The test pins a machine-readable JSON body with keys `runs`, `success`, `failure`, `unstable`, `incidentsOpen`, `incidentsResolved`, `requestsApproved`, `requestsRejected` (whitespace-tolerant `"key": n` matching). → Request: ui-dev to serve the summary as JSON with exactly these keys, or report the actual field names so the test is adjusted.
24. **T-11-05 comment storage assumption (S4)**: SPEC's Incident model has `transitions` and no separate comment list, so `IncidentService.addComment(id, comment)` on a RESOLVED incident is asserted to append an entry to `transitions` (comment + by recorded, status unchanged RESOLVED).
25. **T-SEC-06 switch-toggle GET sub-case (S4)**: there is no plugin-owned toggle endpoint — switches change only through the Jenkins global config POST (S1-covered). The test asserts the real invariants: GET `/configure` renders (200) without flipping either switch, and GET `/configSubmit` is rejected (>=400, Jenkins core `@RequirePOST`) leaving both switch values unchanged.
26. **T-RT-11 assertion shape (S4, D-18)**: the reason (`=1+1`) is a cell of its own in requests.csv, so it is asserted strictly (present and every occurrence preceded by `'`). The parameter payloads must be present in runs.csv; across all four CSVs every payload occurrence is additionally checked to never sit at a raw cell start (position 0 / after a delimiter / after an opening quote) unescaped — occurrences embedded mid-cell (e.g. a `key=value` aggregate) are not formula-injectable and pass, which is exactly the D-18 criterion (cells *starting* with `= + - @`).
27. **T-RT-10 coverage shape (S4 close)**: reason (`<script>alert(...)</script>`) and parameter value (`<img src=x onerror=...>`) payloads are asserted on three render surfaces — request detail (escaped forms present, unescaped absent), dashboard (escaped parameter present after the approved build ran, unescaped absent) and request list (no unescaped payload, no injected DOM element; presence not required there since SPEC does not pin the list's columns). A `CollectingAlertHandler` additionally asserts that no injected script executed on any fetched page. The row's job-name payload variant is unreachable by design: Jenkins core `checkGoodName` rejects `<`/`>` in item names, so no such job can be created. Observed at run time: Jelly's default expression escaping neutralizes `<` (as `&lt;`) and `&` but leaves `>` raw — that is safe (no element can open without a live `<`), so the test accepts both `&lt;script&gt;alert` and `&lt;script>alert` as the escaped form; the no-raw-payload, DOM and alert-handler assertions are unconditional.
28. **P-09 visibility reconciliation audit (Phase 4)**: after the S-01/P-09 visibility model landed, every existing web test was audited against it. NO existing row's Then changed meaning and no fixture needed an extra permission: every actor that reads a request/grant is its requester, its designated approver, a MANAGE/ADMINISTER holder, or holds Item/Read on the target job (e.g. RunRequestWebTest's u2 holds Item/Read everywhere, so T-SEC-02's 403 still isolates the missing Approve permission; GrantWebTest's readers are the requester u1, approver a1, m1 or admin; IncidentTest's rerun caller u1 holds Item/Read; QueueBlockTest's job-page fetches run unsecured or as Request holders). The new visibility rules themselves are covered by T-SEC-08/09 (and S-06/S-07 by T-SEC-10/11).
29. **T-SEC-14 strengthened with a lookup-counting realm (Phase 4, security-02)**: the original row asserted only consistency and post-toggle correctness, which a build with the cache entirely removed would also satisfy. The test now installs a `SecurityRealm` (an `AbstractPasswordBasedSecurityRealm` equivalent to `JenkinsRule.createDummySecurityRealm()`) that increments an `AtomicInteger` on every `loadUserByUsername2`, and measures: (a) the first `isActivated()` does consult the realm (fixture assertion — if it ever stops doing so the row can no longer measure anything and fails loudly rather than silently passing), (b) the second call adds **zero** lookups, (c) after the off/on toggle the next call performs lookups again, (d) and is then cached again. (b) and (c) are the same code path measured with the cache valid and invalid, so the row is falsifiable in both directions. Priority raised P2→P1: this is the /manage page-load DoS against LDAP-backed instances (S-05), not a cosmetic caching detail.
30. **T-SEC-17 fixture route (Phase 4, P-10)**: the empty-scope grant request cannot be produced through the public creation API any more (T-SEC-12 pins that refusal), so the row builds the request with the model factory and writes it to the store directly (`FileStore.saveGrantRequest`) — exactly the "persisted by an older build / hand-edited store file" precondition security-02 S-13c describes. The test asserts the request is loadable and PENDING before approving, so the refusal that follows is the scope re-validation and not a missing record.
31. **Change-window READ paths were uncovered until Phase 5 (T-08-14..18, note for T-08-01 / T-E2E-03)**: SPEC 8 promises that inside an approved window the requester "본인 계정으로 직접 작업"한다 on the target job. The suite asserted only the *write* half of that: T-08-01 (`POST job/X/config.xml` → 200 + persisted), T-09-02/03 (the resulting ChangeRecord and its `grantId`), and the negative rows T-08-02/03/05 (which are denials, so a too-narrow permission answer cannot fail them). Nothing asserted the path a human actually takes — `GET job/X/configure` to open the screen, or `GET job/X/config.xml` to read it. Jenkins core gates **both reads** on `Item.EXTENDED_READ`, a permission core ships **disabled** (`hudson.security.ExtendedReadPermission` off by default) and treats as **implied by** `Item.CONFIGURE`; core's `Job/configure.jelly` layout and `doConfigDotXml`'s GET branch both check it. So an implementation that answers only the exact permission it was asked to confer passes the whole T-08/T-09 block while the change window is unusable in a browser: the user can save a configuration they cannot open. That is what Phase 5 hit against a real Jenkins with 159 green tests. T-08-14/15 pin the two read paths at the HTTP layer, T-08-16 pins the ACL rule itself (`hasPermission(Item.EXTENDED_READ)` must be true inside the window), and T-08-17/18 are the mandatory counterweights — the permission must disappear when the window closes (no permanent leak through the implication walk) and must not widen to another job or to an action the grant does not carry. T-08-16 also carries a **fixture control**: a user holding a plain `Item.CONFIGURE` from the delegate must report `EXTENDED_READ` true, so if core ever stops deriving it the row fails loudly instead of measuring nothing. `GrantConfigureAccessTest` additionally keeps that control as its own method (`directConfigurePermissionOpensAndReadsConfiguration`), which pins that 200 on `configure`/`config.xml` is reachable at all in the resolved Jenkins version — a T-08-14/15 failure is therefore a grant problem, never a broken expectation about core's screens.

32. **Owner scenario rows (`T-OS-*`) and the standing-approval model difference (S-4)**: the `T-OS-<nn>` rows come from six operating scenarios the product owner raised directly (S-1 rejection, S-2 approval↔run traceability, S-3 turning a control on mid-run, S-4 the uncontrolled control group, S-5 configuration change mid-run, S-6 handing a request to another approver) — the same provenance convention as `T-RT-*` for red-team rows, so the owner can trace a row back to the question that produced it. Most of these rows are **coverage confirmation** of behaviour believed to be implemented, not red tests. One model difference must be recorded: the owner described a *standing* approval ("approve once and it keeps running until we put it on hold"), while the current contract binds the approval marker to one requestId and consumes it with a single queue submission (SPEC 6, R-8/D-23) — one approval equals one run, and reuse is blocked (T-RT-02). T-OS-07/08 therefore assert the off→on transition of the control and never that one approval keeps later runs passing. Whether a standing-approval mode should exist is an **owner/SPEC decision**, not a test gap.
33. **S-6 delegation line is out of MVP scope (note for T-OS-10)**: an administrator-configured stand-in/delegation line ("대결") is SPEC item 15 (second release, 순차 결재선), so no test assumes it. The current contract is requester-driven approver change until the decision (SPEC item 3), covered by T-03-04 (change recorded), T-03-05 (no change after the decision) and T-RT-07 (multi-hop audit, superseded approvers refused). T-OS-10 adds the boundary that makes the hand-over safe: an approver on the global list who was never designated on this request cannot decide it.
34. **T-OS-03 asserts the substance of "final", not the wording**: the owner expects the screen to tell the requester that a rejection cannot be resumed. SPEC pins no wording, so the row asserts the rendered REJECTED status, the stored rejection reason visible to the requester, and the absence of an approve path for that request in the page. Confirming the literal wording/visual treatment stays with the Phase 5 e2e pass (same convention as notes 17 and 27).
35. **New API surface used by T-OS-01/02 (`decisionComment`, `decidedAt`)**: the rejection rows read `RunRequest.getDecisionComment()` and `getDecidedAt()`, derived from SPEC section 3's data model (`createdAt, decidedAt, decisionComment`); no earlier test used those two getters. Both were confirmed to exist by compiling the new classes against the plugin's built classes, so the rows are behavioural assertions, not API requests.
36. **In-flight preconditions are gated, not timed (T-OS-05/06/09)**: these rows need a build that is provably running while a setting changes. Each class carries a small test-local `Builder` that blocks on a latch keyed by a persisted gate id (only the id is serialized, so the step survives a job save and the `config.xml` round trip; the latches live in static maps). The gate turns "while running" into an assertion — `isBuilding()` is checked immediately before and after the change — so a timing miss fails loudly instead of passing vacuously, and no `Thread.sleep` is used to wait out a build.

37. **Where D-30's "감사 이력" was read to be, and what SPEC still leaves open (T-06-17/18/19)**: D-30 fixes *that* a blocked marker re-use is recorded and *that* it is queryable on the dashboard and in CSV, but not the record's shape. Derivation used by the rows, from SPEC only: the audit set that SPEC item 12 gives a screen and a CSV export to, and that is neither a run, an incident nor a request, is the change-record set — so the rows read the history through `FileStore.listChangeRecords(YearMonth)` and `batch-control/history/changes.csv`, and require the record to carry a **type of its own** (not one of the eight `ChangeRecord` types SPEC section 3 lists), which is what "별도 레코드" asks for and matches P-04 candidate (a) (`MARKER_REUSE_BLOCKED`-style type). Still undefined in SPEC, and therefore asserted only in substance (text of `target`/`detail`/`diff`, not a field layout): (a) the record's **type name**; (b) which field carries the consumed requestId and the target job — the rows search the record's text rather than naming a field, so any placement passes; (c) whether `ChangeRecord.grantId`/`diff` mean anything here (not asserted); (d) whether a **5th** dataset/CSV endpoint (e.g. `reuse.csv`) would be the intended home instead of `changes.csv` — if core-dev/ui-dev serve it elsewhere, these three rows are adjusted to that endpoint rather than the implementation being wrong about the location; (e) the **actor** attribution: the rows require `user` = the account that replayed the marker (u1's rebuild → u1, u2's revival → u2), reading "누가 시도했는지" as the actor, not the owner of the approval — SPEC does not say this in words. The **falsifiability** of the block hangs on T-06-18: T-06-17 alone is satisfied by an implementation that writes a record on every approved submission, so the clean-run row is mandatory, not decorative. The refusal itself is not re-asserted as a row (T-RT-02 owns it); it appears inside T-06-17/19 only as a fixture control, so the measured record is provably a record of a *blocked* attempt.

38. **D-31 widens D-17, and the creation paths the harness can actually drive (T-08-19..25)**: D-17 gave the `approvalRequired=true` default only to jobs created inside an active grant window (T-RT-05); D-31 makes it every new job while run control is on, whoever creates it and however. The rows therefore cover one creation path each, chosen for the distinct core entry point behind it rather than for the UI label: `createItem` with a `mode` parameter (the New Item form → `createProject`), `createItem` with an XML body (REST → `createProjectFromXML`), CLI `create-job` (the same XML route from outside HTTP), `createItem?mode=copy` (a copy is a new job), a Job DSL seed run (the generator path), and a plain user creating inside a grant window (the D-17 case, kept as a regression). T-08-24 additionally asserts the two outcomes are *equal*, because "the creator does not matter" is the substance of D-31 and not a by-product of the other rows. A multibranch **branch child** is deliberately not among these rows: a computed child cannot be configured through any product path (same reasoning as note 22), so its behaviour is covered on the cause side by T-08-28 instead.
39. **The automation rows guarantee their fixture instead of trusting the default (T-08-26..29)**, and what SPEC still leaves open around D-31: each row in `NewJobAutomationSafetyTest` creates its job through the real `createItem` path with run control on, then **guarantees** the controlled state — D-31 is expected to have applied the default (that is T-08-19..24's assertion, and it is not duplicated here), and if it has not, the fixture applies the property explicitly — and asserts it before firing any cause. So the four rows measure the queue gate, never an unprotected job, and they stay meaningful both before and after D-31 lands: they are the standing safety net for the claim in D-31 that unattended CI is unaffected. Two questions SPEC does not answer and that are therefore **asserted nowhere**: (a) **an explicit setting at creation time** — if a job is created from a config.xml that carries the plugin property with `approvalRequired=false`, SPEC 8/D-31 do not say whether that stated intent survives or the default wins; no row pins either behaviour, and the point needs an owner decision before one is added; (b) **retroactivity** — the D-31 criterion speaks about jobs that are newly created ("새로 생성되는 모든 잡"), and nothing is asserted about a job that already existed when run control was switched on (the closest existing coverage is T-OS-07, which pins that such a job keeps building until its property is switched on by hand).
 **Superseded in part (D-34, note 46):** T-08-26 and T-08-27 no longer assert that a *newly created* job builds from a timer or an upstream call — D-34 reverses that contract — and were rewritten to assert the unlock path instead. T-08-28 and T-08-29 keep their meaning, and the "guarantee the fixture, never trust the default" technique described here is unchanged; the fixture now states all three switches instead of `approvalRequired` alone.

40. **`T-UI-*` rows pin a screen contract the implementation defined, not a SPEC acceptance criterion (T-UI-01..05)**: docs/SPEC.md contains no acceptance criterion about a recent-run table on the approval screen, about a `runs` query parameter or about a post-approval notice. ui-dev added the run-count control in commit `d6c0878` and reported the contract these rows freeze: `runs` accepts 5/10/20/50 and defaults to 5; every other value (`100000`, `-1`, `0`, `7`, `abc`, empty, oversized numbers) silently falls back to 5 with no error page; no `runs` value may produce run rows when the viewer cannot read the target job or the job has been deleted (P-09 must not be bypassable); and the post-approval notice appears only while the request is `APPROVED` with `executedRunId == null`. The rows therefore protect the current screen behaviour from silent regressions — they are **not evidence that SPEC requires any of it**. A person should decide whether the recent-run window and its allow-list belong in SPEC item 5/10; if the answer is no, these rows are dropped with the feature rather than defended. Two deliberate limits: (a) only the two P-09 rows are P0 (T-UI-03/04) — disclosure is a security property, while the window size is a convenience; (b) the notice's literal text and markup are not asserted, because nothing pins them (same convention as notes 17, 27 and 34), so T-UI-05 asserts the state the notice is keyed on instead. → Request: ui-dev to give the notice a stable marker (e.g. `id="batch-control-approved-notice"`) or report its literal text, and T-UI-05 will then assert the notice itself rather than its state.
41. **A Windows-only temp-directory flake can redden a whole class (observed 2026-09-26 on `RunRequestServiceTest.t_03_05`)**: on Windows a JenkinsRule temporary directory occasionally cannot be deleted because a file handle is still open, and the teardown failure is reported against whichever test the runner was on (t_03_05 in the observed run) although the product is fine — re-running that class alone passed 14/14. Before investigating a single red row in this suite, re-run its class on its own; if it goes green, this was the cause and there is nothing to fix.

42. **D-31 makes fixture ORDER load-bearing: a job created while run control is on already carries a `BatchControlJobProperty`, so a later `addProperty` is silently shadowed.** Jenkins core `Job#addProperty` *appends* a property instead of replacing one of the same class, and `Job#getProperty(Class)` returns the **first** match. So the once-standard fixture — enable run control, create the job, then `addProperty(new BatchControlJobProperty(...))` — now installs a second property that the plugin never reads: `approvalRequired` still looks right (both copies say true), while `blockTimer`, `blockUpstream`, `allowedUpstreamJobs` and `jobApprovers` quietly have no effect and the row measures nothing. The same default also means a job created under run control is **not** an "uncontrolled job" fixture any more. Both traps were hit for real on 2026-09-26 (13 rows: T-06-05/07/12/16, T-RT-01, T-05-06, T-OS-05..09, T-10-01/03) — all fixture rot, no product defect. Fixtures therefore either create the job **before** `setRunControlEnabled(true)`, or go through `BatchControlFixtures` (src/test): `setBatchControl(job, property)` removes any existing copy before adding and then asserts the installed instance is the one `getProperty` returns, and `uncontrolled(job)` strips every copy for a "run control is on but this job is not controlled" premise. Prefer the creation-order variant when the fixture must not produce an extra save of the job (an extra save is an extra `CONFIGURE` record while change control is on — see `OwnerScenarioConfigChangeTest`). **The most valuable find of that repair: two rows were passing while measuring nothing.** `T-06-04` (Replay is blocked) and `T-06-11` (an allow-listed upstream job passes) install their settings the shadowed way, so the plugin read the D-31 default instead — `approvalRequired=true` happened to be the same in both copies, so the row still went green, while the setting the row exists to exercise (`blockUpstream` + `allowedUpstreamJobs`, and "the job was uncontrolled for its first build") had no effect at all. **Green is therefore not evidence that a fixture took effect here:** whenever a row's premise is a job-level setting other than plain `approvalRequired`, assert that the property the plugin reads back is the one the fixture installed — which is what `BatchControlFixtures.setBatchControl` now does for every call site, so the trap cannot reappear silently.
 **Extended by D-34 (note 46):** the property a job created under run control carries now also says `blockTimer=true` and `blockUpstream=true`, so the trap is no longer limited to settings a fixture wanted to *add* — a fixture that creates a job while run control is on and then relies on a **timer or upstream cause passing** measures a blocked job unless it states the switches through `BatchControlFixtures.setBatchControl` (or takes the job out of control with `uncontrolled`). Four fixtures were hardened for this ahead of the implementation: `IncidentTest` t_11_01 (`uncontrolled`), t_11_02 and t_11_06 (stated property), `HistoryWebTest` t_12_04 and `OwnerScenarioApprovalTraceTest` t_os_04. No Then changed — each row keeps measuring what it measured; only the job state it measures on is now stated rather than inherited.

43. **A `<form>` on a read-only screen leaks the crumb into the URL, and core gives no opt-out (T-UI-06)**: while the approval screen's recent-run size control was a `<select>` inside a `<form>`, Jenkins core's own JavaScript added a crumb to **every** form on the page, so choosing a size navigated to `…/?runs=20&Jenkins-Crumb=41855d…&json=%7B…%7D` — reproduced in a browser against a real Jenkins in Phase 5, not inferred. A crumb in a GET query is a CSRF token written into browser history, proxy logs and `Referer` headers, and there is no core flag to suppress the injection, so the fix was structural: ui-dev replaced the form with a group of `<a href="?runs=10">` links (`e98d211`). T-UI-06 therefore pins the **structure and the outcome together** — a plain link per allowed size, a working navigation (HTTP 200 + the chosen window rendered, so a dead link cannot pass), and a landing URL free of `Jenkins-Crumb` and `json=`. Deliberately independent of HtmlUnit: the row does not rely on the test browser reproducing core's injection, because a reverted implementation has no per-size links at all and fails on the first assertion. The property the removed `<select>` used to carry — "the size in effect is visible" — is not dropped with it: the row asserts that exactly one link is marked `aria-current` and that it is the one just followed. Note that no test ever asserted `selected="selected"`, so nothing had to be migrated; the property was only ever covered inside ui-dev's own markup until this row.

44. **P-03 is resolved (owner ruling, Phase 5): the masked-storage behaviour stays, and the limitation it costs is now a pinned acceptance criterion (T-SEC-07, T-SEC-19).** The owner chose option ① of P-03 — a secret parameter's value is masked *before* it is handed to the request store, so no plaintext is persisted anywhere, and the price is that **a job with a secret parameter cannot reproduce the original secret when the approved run executes**. That is an accepted limitation stated in the README, not a defect, and T-SEC-07 asserts it explicitly (the build receives `********`) so that a later "convenience" change which starts persisting a recoverable secret fails the row instead of quietly passing it. The owner's reasoning is worth keeping next to the row: the approver decides by reading the raw parameters on the request screen, and showing secrets in clear there would be the larger problem. **Why the row drives the browser form instead of the service API**: P-03 states the masking happens where the submitted `ParameterValue`s are converted into the stored `Map<String,String>` — the service API receives strings and can no longer tell which of them was sensitive, so a row that called `RunRequestService.create(job, plaintextMap, …)` would be asserting against a layer that has no way to comply. Two consequences follow for anyone adding a new request-creation path later (SPEC item 14's REST API, a CLI command, an automated re-run): the caller must mask before calling, because the store will not do it for them, and such a path needs its own T-SEC-07-shaped row. **Why the row is falsifiable in both directions**: every "no plaintext" assertion has a positive twin on the same surface using a second, non-sensitive parameter of the same job, whose typed value must be present verbatim (stored, rendered, exported, passed to the build) — so "store nothing" fails; the secret parameter's *name* must be present wherever the plain one is — so "drop the secret parameter" fails; and the store scan additionally asserts that the non-sensitive value really is found in a file under `$JENKINS_HOME/batch-control/`, so it cannot pass by scanning an empty or wrong directory. The row also forbids the Password parameter's **definition default** as a second plaintext: Jenkins core renders a concealed password control with the `<DEFAULT>` sentinel rather than the stored value, so the submission either carries the value the test typed or resolves to the definition default, and both are non-empty and both are forbidden — the row therefore cannot be satisfied by an empty submission whichever path core takes. Verified by mutation: submitting a different value for the non-sensitive parameter reddens the row at the "stored verbatim" assertion, which proves the typed values really travel form → store.

45. **T-E2E-06 moved the acceptance criterion to where D-33 puts it, without lowering it, and stays at the e2e layer.** The old row demanded an expiry notice *in the denial screen itself* plus a re-request link there; D-33 rules that screen out (Jenkins core hardcodes the 403 handler in `SecurityRealm#createFilterImpl`, so it is not an extension point, and the work-arounds — overriding core's `accessDenied.jelly` from plugin resources, or filtering 403s in a `PluginServletFilter` — would make this plugin intercept **every** permission denial on the instance, a jenkinsci hosting review rejection). The requirement therefore moves to the grants screen (`/batch-control/grants/`: remaining time of an active window, history of expired windows, re-request link), and the row now checks **both** halves: that the denial screen is core's own and untouched (including no restoration of the values being edited, which D-33 excludes because keeping a change that failed the permission check contradicts change control), and that the guidance is reachable where D-33 puts it. **Layer decision — it stays `e2e`**: half (a) is an assertion about what the plugin does *not* install into an instance-wide screen, which is only meaningfully observed in a browser against a real Jenkins; its automatable core (denial from the first permission check after expiry, no active grant, no leak through implied permissions) is already pinned deterministically by T-08-17/18 in `GrantConfigureAccessTest`, so moving the row to `integration` would duplicate that and add nothing. Half (b) is the content of a plugin screen for which SPEC fixes only the three items D-33 names and no markup; pinning exact markup at the integration layer would freeze a screen contract SPEC does not state (the `T-UI-*` rows exist precisely for contracts the implementation defined, and this one has not been reported yet). If ui-dev reports the grants-screen contract, half (b) should become an integration row (`T-UI-*`) and this row keeps only half (a). **The existing e2e harness already reaches both halves, so only the expectation moves** (e2e/** is e2e-tester's path, so this is a request, not an edit): `e2e/scripts/rest-grant-configure.sh` step 6 currently greps the post-expiry refusal body for `expired` / `grant` / `batch-control/grants` / `Request` / `permission` and treats their absence as the failure — under D-33 that body is core's screen and those needles are *expected* to be absent there, while the same needles belong on `/batch-control/grants/`, which `e2e/scripts/rest-screens-v2.sh` already fetches and already checks for the `Remaining` column. Step 6 should therefore assert core's untouched denial (and that the refused save left the configuration unchanged, which step 5 already prints) and hand the guidance needles to the grants-screen script.
46. **D-34 separates creating a job from operating it, and what SPEC still leaves open (T-08-30..36, the rewrites of T-08-26/27 and T-RT-05)**: D-34 adds `blockTimer=true` and `blockUpstream=true` to the new-job default D-31 established, so that *the act of creating a job starts nothing*. Reading used for the rows, from SPEC item 8 / D-34 only: (1) the default applies to the same set of creations D-31 applies to — every path, every creator, while **run control** is on (hence the same path coverage: New Item form, REST `createItem`, CLI `create-job`, copy, Job DSL seed) — and D-32's computed children stay excluded (T-10-07); (2) the substance is asserted twice, as the stored switches and as the refused cause, because SPEC item 6's queue gate is where a switch becomes observable: T-08-31 for the timer door and T-08-33 for the upstream door; (3) the way out D-34 names ("turn the switch off in the job configuration") is asserted as its own pair of rows — T-08-26/27 — because a control that cannot be released would make every generated job permanently dead; the unlock is expressed through the job property, the same convention T-OS-05/06 use for an administrator flipping a job switch, and the ChangeRecord it produces is SPEC item 9's and is covered by T-09-01/02, so it is not re-asserted here. **Two rows were rewritten rather than relaxed.** T-08-26/27 asserted that a *newly created* job still builds from a timer and from an upstream call — that was the D-31 safety net, and D-34 deliberately reverses it. The property those rows were really protecting is that a job's automatic triggers can be live, so they now measure it after the switches are turned off; the width of D-34 is bounded instead by T-08-28 (the SCM cause is untouched) and its intended effect by T-08-29 (the human path stays refused). T-RT-05 went the other way: its original R-2 demand *was* D-34 ("a change window must not be usable to plant a run path that outlives it"), and note 19 had narrowed it to the manual path because D-17 promised no more; the cron assertion is restored. **Red state when committed (implementation is D-31 only):** 7 rows fail — T-08-30, T-08-32, T-08-35 and T-08-36 on `blockTimer` (`expected: <true> but was: <false>`, the created property carries only `approvalRequired=true`); T-RT-05 on the same value for the planted job; T-08-31 behaviourally first, the cron firing is admitted (`expected: <null> but was: <hudson.model.queue.FutureImpl@...>`); T-08-33 behaviourally, the upstream call builds the new job (`expected: <null> but was: <rest-downstream #1>`). Measured on the branch with `mvn -o test`: **204 tests / 7 failures / 0 errors** (the 197-test baseline plus the seven new methods; every other class green, including the four hardened fixtures). Already green and expected to stay green: T-08-34 (run control off locks nothing — the guard), the rewritten T-08-26/27 (the unlock path works today because the switches default to open), T-08-28, T-08-29 and all of T-08-19..25. **Questions SPEC does not answer, and which are therefore asserted nowhere:** (a) **an explicit switch value in the creation payload** — a config.xml, CLI, Job DSL or copy source that says `blockTimer=false`/`blockUpstream=false`, or that carries an `allowedUpstreamJobs` list. P-13 settled this for `approvalRequired` (the default wins, the rest of the property is carried over verbatim), which would mean the creator's trigger policy survives — the opposite answer for the two new switches; the fixtures here carry `<triggers>` but no plugin property, so the suite admits either reading, exactly as note 39 (a) left it for D-31. A Job DSL seed pinning `blockTimer: false` is the practical case. (b) **The unlock is change-controlled only while change control is on.** D-34 justifies the lock by saying the release "goes through a permission window and a record", but the lock is conditioned on **run** control; with run control on and change control off there is no grant window at all, so anyone holding Item/Configure releases the job freely (the change is still recorded — SPEC item 9 — but not gated). No row asserts a gate that SPEC does not promise, and T-08-34 only covers run control off. (c) **A copy inherits nothing** — T-08-35 reads a copy as a new job by analogy with T-08-22 (D-31); D-34 does not mention copies. (d) **Nothing about visibility**: SPEC pins no indication on the job screen that a job was created locked, nor any guidance naming the two switches when a timer/upstream cause is refused — unlike the human path, where SPEC item 6 forbids a silent failure, an unattended refusal is silent by contract (T-06-07), so a locked generated job looks to an operator exactly like a job whose cron never fires. (e) **Approving the activation** is explicitly out: D-34 records as a known limitation that the approver of a change window approves the window, not the job's operation, so no row asserts an activation approval.

47. **S-16's rows assert rows and totals, never "HTTP 200 came back" (T-SEC-20..26, and the sibling S-22 rows T-SEC-27..31).** The defect being pinned answered 200 with an empty table and a footer reading `Page 1 ( requests)`: `Jenkins.getItemByFullName` signals "you may discover this but not read it" by **throwing** `AccessDeniedException`, and Jelly's JEXL evaluator swallows that into a blank value, so one request filed against a discover-only job blanked every row of `/batch-control/requests/` including the caller's own. A row asserting only the status code - or only that something is refused - passes against exactly that build, which is the shape of mistake that let E2E-D1 through 159 integration tests. So every row names the records that must be on the screen, the footer total as a number, and the record that must be absent, with an administrator's view of the same store as the false-positive guard. Falsifiability was verified by running these rows against the parent commits (`8b552c8` for S-16/N-01/U-01/U-02, `e6f84e0` for S-22) in a throwaway worktree: T-SEC-20/22/23/26, T-SEC-27..31 and T-UI-07/09/10/11/12/13/14/15/16/18 all fail there. **T-SEC-21 and T-UI-08/17 pass pre-fix by design** - they are control rows, labelled as such in the table, and must never be read as the regression pin for their finding. T-UI-19/20/21 cannot be run pre-fix at all: the helpers they call did not exist, which is their own form of falsification.
48. **The N-01, U-01, U-02 and U-07 rows are `T-UI-*` screen contracts, not acceptance criteria (T-UI-07..21).** SPEC names no HTTP status code for a rejected submission, no sidebar entry for change control, and nothing about diff rendering or time vocabulary; SPEC item 6's only sidebar sentence ("Build Now" is replaced by "Request Run") is already pinned at page level by T-06-15, which keeps passing unchanged. These rows therefore freeze what the implementation decided, on the same footing as T-UI-01..06 (note 40), and are dropped with the feature if a human rejects the contract. Two of them are meant to be read as pairs rather than alone: T-UI-12 is written so that it can only be read as measuring condition 3 of the sidebar entry - it re-asserts conditions 1 and 2 unchanged at every step and keeps a job outside the grant scope as the isolation control - because that is the condition most likely to rot; and T-UI-16/17 are a positive/negative pair, since a caption assertion alone cannot tell a plugin label from core's own.
49. **Open question found while writing T-SEC-26, deliberately left unasserted: the incident screen links a build of a job the caller may not read.** `IncidentItem.getRunUrl()` builds the Run cell's href from the stored run id with no permission check, so a `ViewHistory` holder who holds only `Item/Discover` on the job gets a live `job/<name>/<number>/` anchor, while `RequestItem.getExecutedRunUrl()` deliberately renders plain text in exactly that case ("or when the job is not visible to the caller (P-09)"). Nothing is disclosed that the page does not already print as text - the job full name and the run number are fields of the incident record - so this is a dead link that looks like a live one rather than a leak, which is the family of problem U-02 was filed about. Two screens of the same plugin disagree about the same contract, so the ruling belongs to the maintainer: T-SEC-26 asserts only the boundary that is not in dispute (the caller is still refused `job/J/` and `job/J/1/` themselves) and the test body records why the link itself is asserted in neither direction.

50. **Hosting-review rows T-02-06..09 (D-41, #31) and the T-SEC-11 status change.** T-02-06 uses matrix-auth's `PermissionFinder` as the oracle because it is the lookup matrix-auth's JCasC support calls, and it resolves the part before the slash against `PermissionGroup#getId()` — so the row measures the group id, not the title; the display-title spelling `Batch Control/<Name>` is the guard that fails if the title-derived id is still in place. T-02-07..09 pair every "absent" assertion with a user for whom the same link is shown and the same URL answers 200. **T-SEC-11 was changed from 403 to 404** because SPEC item 2 now says the per-job action "is absent, not merely refused … its URL answers 404"; this follows a contract change made in SPEC, it is not a loosening. **Sub-URL ruling (SPEC 2, commit 1986644):** an absent action is absent at every URL beneath it. So **T-SEC-15** (POST `job/X/batch-control/submit` without Request) moved from 403 to 404, and **T-12-01** now expects 404 at `batch-control/history/` and `batch-control/incidents/` for a user with no Batch Control permission. Its counterpart (a Request-only user still gets 403 from those sections; a ViewHistory holder gets 200) is asserted in the same row, and T-02-08 relies on the same 403 as its premise. **Four further rows moved from 403 to 404 for the same reason, where the failing actor turned out to hold none of the Batch Control permissions at all** (root-level URLs beneath `/batch-control/`, not job-scoped): **T-08-13** (GrantWebTest, u0), **T-10-05** (RunRecordListenerTest, nohist), **T-12-05** (HistoryWebTest, nohist) and **T-SEC-02** (RunRequestWebTest, u2 — Jenkins/Read + Item/Read only, no Batch Control permission). This supersedes the T-SEC-02 half of **note 28**, which was written before the SPEC 2 / #31 contract change and had assumed 403 was still correct for u2; note 28's audit of the *other* rows (GrantWebTest's readers, IncidentTest's u1, QueueBlockTest) is unaffected because those actors hold at least one Batch Control permission. GrantWebTest's `revokePostWithoutManageIs403` (untagged, no matrix ID) was likewise split for the same reason: u1/a1 (each holding some Batch Control permission but not Manage) still get 403, only u0 (zero Batch Control permissions) moved to 404.
51. **D-35a/b/c rows T-02-10..33 (#30, per-strategy subclasses) and what changed around them.** The rows port PoC-5 rows 1-8, 14 and 15 (option A) plus row 9 on both strategies (T-02-12, T-02-16: grant expiry is P0 by the matrix rule) onto the frozen names `BatchControlMatrixAuthorizationStrategy` / `BatchControlRoleBasedAuthorizationStrategy`. Priority: P0 for PoC rows 1, 2, 14, 15 (T-02-10, 11, 22, 24, 25), for the two grant-expiry rows, for the legacy matrix load (T-02-26: an upgrade that mis-loads the strategy locks out or opens up the whole instance) and for the migrate/revert refusals (T-02-32, a 403 row); P1 otherwise. **Contract changes, not loosenings:** (a) every existing test that installed `new BatchControlAuthorizationStrategy(delegate)` now installs the matrix subclass with the same entries, because D-35a withdrew the wrapper as an API (GrantConfigureAccessTest, GrantServiceTest, GrantWebTest, GrantWindowAbuseTest, ChangeRecordTest, GrantRestartTest, NewJobApprovalDefaultTest, JobGrantSidebarEntryTest, GrantMonitorsTest T-08-06, SecurityRegressionTest T-SEC-14; the two `MockAuthorizationStrategy` delegates became matrix entries granted globally, which is what `.everywhere()` meant); (b) **T-SEC-13 is retired**: its assertion was about constructing the wrapper, which no longer exists; the attack it guarded (a nested wrapper) is re-asserted at load time as T-02-29. **Legacy XML fixture:** the tests never reference the withdrawn class in Java; they save the plain strategy and rewrite the saved `<authorizationStrategy>` element into `<authorizationStrategy class="…BatchControlAuthorizationStrategy"><delegate class="<plain>">…</delegate></authorizationStrategy>`, keeping the plain body byte for byte, then `Jenkins.reload()`. **Left unasserted on purpose:** the HTTP status of the reverted self-grant POST in T-02-22 (D-35b says the property is restored, not that the request fails), whether the rest of that save persists, and the JCasC *apply* of the Batch Control global configuration (T-02-20 asserts its export only; its YAML symbol is not in SPEC). **Open SPEC conflict, reported, not decided here:** SPEC 8 still says "Role Strategy selected → JIT-unsupported notice" and "구현: 위임형 AuthorizationStrategy"; with D-35a both are stale. T-08-08 is kept unchanged (plain `RoleBasedAuthorizationStrategy`, where the notice is still true) until a human rules.
52. **#30 follow-ups (SPEC commits 29246f4, 092b319).** The SPEC 8 conflict in note 51 is resolved: the "JIT unsupported" notice is replaced by the `batch-control-strategy` monitor, so **T-08-08 is rewritten** to that line (a contract change in SPEC, not a loosening); no test references `RoleStrategyNoticeMonitor` any more. The D-35c line now covers the creation payload: T-02-34 (config.xml payload) and T-02-35 (copy) are P0 rows that assert the property is removed and recorded; T-02-36 pins the SPEC 8 sentence on a wrapper around the global matrix. T-02-37 pins the revert button. **T-02-23 fixture fix:** its "ordinary save" edited nothing, because a new job's config.xml has no `<description>` element, so the replace was a no-op and the row failed on the description readback. The payload now inserts a description after the root element, and a premise asserts that the payload differs from the saved config. No assertion was removed. **T-02-13:** role-strategy's `table.js` does not parse in HtmlUnit, so that WebClient runs without JavaScript. The row now asserts more markup than before: both sub-page links, and that the Manage Roles page renders the installed `team-.*` role.
53. **security-05 regression rows T-02-38..43 and the D-35d contract changes.** S-01: T-02-38 (inherited folder grant) and T-02-39 (Create-grant Configure stops at what the holder created). S-02: T-02-41. S-03: T-02-42/43 run a real Jenkins without each optional plugin; their classes reference no type of the omitted plugin, and the legacy XML fixture moved to `LegacyWrapperXml`, which uses core and JDK types only. S-05: T-02-40. Priorities follow the coordinator's instruction: S-01, S-03 and S-04 rows are P0 (T-02-36, 38, 39, 42, 43). S-02 (T-02-41) and S-05 (T-02-40) are P1, because D-35d resolves S-02 with a monitor warning and security-05 rates S-05 MEDIUM. **Contract changes, not loosenings:** (a) **T-02-36 is reversed** by D-35d(3): a wrapper around the global matrix is now unwrapped, not converted, and the row adds the S-04 regression (a stale property stays ineffective). (b) D-35d(2) gives the `batch-control-strategy` monitor a second trigger (no `QueueItemAuthenticator`). Every row that asserts the monitor is *quiet* for a Batch Control strategy (T-02-17, T-02-26, T-02-30, T-02-33, T-02-37, T-08-08) now configures a `MockQueueItemAuthenticator` first, through `StrategyFixtures.configureBuildAuthenticator()`. This isolates the strategy half the row measures, and no assertion was removed. The authenticator half is measured on its own by T-02-41.
54. **Plugin interaction rows T-06-20..42 and T-09-16..18 (#34, #36).** One class per third-party plugin (`PluginInteraction*Test`, shared fixture `PluginInteractionFixtures`) so that a slow plugin does not slow the others. Every blocking row runs as u1 who holds **Job/Build** and Request — without Job/Build core refuses the build URL with 403 on its own and the row would measure core's permission check instead of the gate — and uses the standard triple. Every blocking row has a false-positive guard proving the plugin's path really schedules a build when the job is not controlled (T-06-21/26/29/32/35/41), or an approved twin proving the run still starts (T-06-23/30/37/39/42). customize-build-now does not add a URL of its own: it relabels core's build link through `AlternativeUiTextProvider` (job property `BuildNowTextProperty`), so "the customized build URL" is core's `job/<name>/build` reached through the relabelled anchor. T-06-20 asserts only that a "Request Run" entry into `batch-control` is still offered, not which caption the relabelled core entry ends up with — SPEC does not say who wins the caption. parameterized-trigger runs carry an `UpstreamCause`, so they follow the existing `blockUpstream` semantics (D-16): T-06-27/28 blocked with `blockUpstream=true`, T-06-29 passes by default. The configuration-as-code row of issue #36 is out of this slice (T-02-20/21 already cover the strategy round-trip).
55. **Naginator retries (T-06-33/34) — derived from D-23, automatic case flagged for human confirmation.** SPEC item 6 says "an approved run is queued exactly once" and D-23 that the approval marker is consumed by one queue entry and that reuse by requeue or rebuild is blocked. Naginator copies the failed build's causes into the retry, so a retry of an approved run is a second queue entry of the same approval: the manual Retry (T-06-33) is refused — it is also a manual run without an approved request, so SPEC settles it. The automatic retry (T-06-34) is refused under the same two sentences, but an operator may reasonably expect naginator to retry a failed batch without a new approval; SPEC as written does not allow it, and the row is written to SPEC. If the owner wants automatic retries to pass, that is a SPEC change (a named pass-through like `blockTimer`), not a test change. Whether the refused retry also writes a `MARKER_REUSE_BLOCKED` record (D-30) depends on how the marker travels, which the test cannot know without `src/main`; the rows therefore assert the blocking triple only. Observed at commit time: T-06-33 is green and T-06-34 is red (the automatic retry runs as build #2), so the manual and the automatic path are treated differently today.
56. **No-op save rows T-09-12..15 (#20).** Each "no record" row asserts its premise through a test `SaveableListener` (`@TestExtension`) that counts Jenkins save events per config file: a save that never fired cannot make the row green. T-09-13 uses a Pipeline job because Freestyle config.xml carries no `plugin=` attribute on its own. A POSTed config.xml is not kept verbatim by core (observed), so the `@1.0` state is written to disk and loaded with `Jenkins.reload()`; the row asserts the reloaded file still holds `@1.0` and that the following plain `save()` differs from it only in the version attributes, so that save is exactly the "saved unchanged after a plugin upgrade" case of the SPEC line. T-09-14 uses a test `SCMSource` (`MetadataSCMSource`, no heads, one stateless metadata action per fetch) because the scm-api test jar (`MockSCMSource`) is not a test dependency and an unchanged `SingleSCMSource` makes branch-api save nothing but the computation. Observed with branch-api 2.1303: indexing saves `indexing.xml` always and `state.xml` when the folder-level source actions change, and calls `save()` on the project's config.xml only when migrating legacy folder-level actions — so the premise counts save events of any file directly in the project's root directory (at least one per indexing run) and asserts config.xml unchanged modulo version attributes. A listener that treats the computation or state save as a configuration save of the folder is what this row catches; organization folders are not exercised (no navigator is available in test scope). A UI form resubmission without edits is deliberately **not** a row: with throttle-concurrents and other plugins on the test classpath a form submit can add default-valued job properties that were not in the programmatically created job, which is a real configuration change. T-09-15 is the false-positive guard (a normaliser that dropped everything would fail it).
57. **security-07 regression rows T-SEC-32..34 (S-01, S-02, S-04) and two literal scenarios that turned out to be unreachable.** T-SEC-32 (S-01) reproduces cleanly as the report describes it and is **red** against the reviewed build: a Pipeline job's own approved run self-triggering a second queue entry of itself is refused even with `blockUpstream=false`, because the queue decision handler unwraps a same-job `UpstreamCause` as if it were a marker replay. T-SEC-33 (S-02) and T-SEC-34 (S-04) could not be written to the coordinator's literal wording, for two independent, experimentally-verified reasons, and were adapted; both adaptations keep the underlying SPEC-9 property the finding is about, and both are recorded here rather than silently narrowed. **S-02:** the coordinator's scenario asks for a single-quoted XML *attribute* value embedding `plugin="1.0"`. No field in this plugin or in any of its test-scope dependencies (workflow-job, workflow-multibranch, branch-api, parameterized-trigger, naginator, lockable-resources, throttle-concurrents, authorize-project, jobConfigHistory, customize-build-now, rebuild, build-token-root, job-dsl, cloudbees-folder — scanned for the `@XStreamAsAttribute` annotation directly in their jars, zero matches) writes a free-text value as an XML attribute; and even a fixture-invented attribute with no backing field does not survive to be diffed at all, because `AbstractItem#updateByXml` re-serialises the item from its live object model on every save — an unbacked attribute is silently absent from the very next stored file (verified: POSTing `<project data='cmd plugin="1.0" tail'>` on a fresh FreeStyle job stores back a plain `<project>` with no trace of `data`, and the transition is recorded as zero CONFIGURE records, which would be true of *any* implementation, buggy or fixed — the row would measure nothing). T-SEC-33 instead carries the same substring through `description`, a real field that round-trips byte for byte (T-09-12/15 rely on the same fact); the literal quotes end up XML-entity-escaped on disk (`plugin=&quot;custom-thing@1.0&quot;`, also verified experimentally — Jenkins's writer escapes quotes in element text, not only in attribute values), which the row's edit is expressed in terms of. This substitution is **not equivalent** to the reported line-34-35/104 defect if that code iterates DOM attributes specifically (S-02's own text: "match inside another **attribute's** value") rather than scanning raw or decoded text irrespective of node kind — and observationally, **T-SEC-33 currently passes** against the reviewed build with both the report's literal `plugin="1.0"` shape and T-09-13's stricter `name@version` shape, meaning this adaptation does not reproduce whatever S-02 found. It is kept as a real, currently-satisfied regression guard (ordinary text resembling a plugin attribute is not over-matched), not as the S-02 pin. **Request: security-reviewer confirm whether `ConfigNormalizer` (`listener/ConfigNormalizer.java:34-35,:104`) processes parsed XML attributes specifically or raw/decoded text irrespective of node kind — the answer decides whether T-SEC-33 needs a different field or already is the regression pin — or provide/point to a real shipping field (any plugin, any version) whose value is written as a bare XML attribute, since the report itself found none.** **S-04:** the coordinator's scenario is a direct config.xml POST (`Item/Configure` over HTTP) to an existing branch job of a multibranch project. Verified experimentally: workflow-multibranch's `BranchJobProperty` gives every branch job an ACL (`BranchJobProperty$1`) that denies `Item/CONFIGURE` unconditionally, even to a user holding `Jenkins/Administer` (`hasPermission` returns `false`); the same is true one level up, for an organization folder's generated multibranch project child (`MultiBranchProject$1`, same experiment, same result). SPEC item 9 names exactly these two computed-folder types (multibranch project, organization folder) and no other is available in test scope, so an HTTP-POST-shaped reproduction of S-04 is not reachable for either. T-SEC-34 instead uses a direct, non-indexing `save()` on the branch child — the Job DSL / script shape SPEC item 9 separately requires to be recorded, and the one path of the four (UI, REST, CLI, Job DSL) actually reachable for this item type — and **is red**: the direct edit persists (the fixture premise) but writes no CONFIGURE record, reproducing the finding's substance ("takes effect ... and is never recorded") through a reachable path. **Request: security-reviewer confirm S-04 was verified reachable by some path this investigation did not find (for example a permission grant this plugin's own `BatchControlMatrixAuthorizationStrategy` might restore on a branch child that core's ACL alone would not) — if not, the report's illustrative wording should be corrected to name the Job DSL/script path, which is what T-SEC-34 now pins.**

58. **D-36, D-37, D-40 rows T-03-07..21, T-08-37..46, T-13-01..19 (#32, #33, #35).** The rows drive the frozen form contract (repeated `approvers`, `createNamePattern`) through HTTP (`ApproverFormFixtures`), so they measure what a browser or script submits. Refusals are asserted as 4xx rather than exactly 403, because a request's detail URL may answer 404 to a user who cannot see it. The legacy row T-03-21 makes its fixture by rewriting a file the current plugin wrote into the pre-D-37 shape and asserts that premise before the restart. Notification rows allow asynchronous delivery (SPEC: a notifier never delays the action): expected events are polled for up to 10 s, forbidden ones are checked after a 1.5 s quiet period; no row waits for an expiry in real time. Open points for the owner: (a) SPEC mentions a REST/JSON view keeping an `approver` field, but no such endpoint is defined, so only the compatibility getter `getApprover()` is pinned; (b) for GRANT_EXPIRING the notification's `requestId` is taken to be the grant request id; (c) CSV header names are compared case-insensitively because SPEC names the columns but not their case. Existing tests adjusted to the renamed field (`approver` -> `approvers`), assertions unchanged: SecurityRegressionTest (T-SEC-15 submit), SecretParameterMaskingTest form helper (now also checkboxes), DiscoverOnlyRequestScreenTest change-approver control (select or checkboxes), RequestParameterValidationTest JSON form.


59. **GrantRequest gets its own `changeApprover` endpoint (rows T-03-22..26, coordinator instructions 2026-09-28).** D-26 (the requester may change the designation before a decision) and D-37's recording contract (from set, to set, by, at) were pinned for `RunRequest` only (T-03-17..19); the coordinator instructed a symmetric `POST batch-control/grants/<id>/changeApprover` for `GrantRequest`, with repeated `approvers`, restricted to the requester and to the PENDING state. The rows reuse `MultiApproverTest`'s existing grant fixtures (`submitGrantOk`, `decideGrant`) and a new `ApproverFormFixtures.changeGrantApprovers` helper next to `changeRunApprovers`. Unlike note 58's general 4xx looseness, T-03-23..26 pin the exact codes named (404, 405, 403). **Expected API note:** these rows assume `GrantRequest.getApproverChanges()` / `GrantRequest.ApproverChange` with the same shape as `RunRequest`'s (`getFromApprovers()`, `getToApprovers()`, `getBy()`, `getAt()`); this is confirmed, not merely requested, by `docs/SPEC.md` section 3 (commit 70d1b02) and `GrantRequest.ApproverChange` (commit bd54fb2) landing after the first version of these rows. **P-09 ruling (coordinator, 2026-09-28, supersedes the first version of T-03-23):** the request-visibility model (DECISIONS P-09) hides a grant request from anyone who is not `Manage`, its requester or a designated approver — non-visible detail/action URLs answer 404, not 403, the same rule the run-request rows already apply elsewhere. T-03-23 was first written expecting 403 for u2 (a `BatchControl/RequestGrant` holder who is neither requester nor a designated approver of *this* request) reasoning that holding *some* Batch Control permission rules out SPEC 2's 404 case (note 50); the ruling corrects this: note 50's 404 case is about holding **no** Batch Control permission at all, while P-09's is about not being able to **see this particular request**, a narrower, per-record visibility rule that applies regardless of which permissions the caller holds elsewhere. T-03-23 now pins 404 for u2. **T-03-26 is new**: it separates the two refusal reasons by using a1, a designated approver of the same request (so P-09 makes it visible, 200/404 is not in question) who is still not its requester, and pins that the ownership check on top of visibility is a 403, not a 404 — the pairing that makes T-03-23's 404 provably about visibility and not merely "any non-requester is refused."

60. **security-08 regression rows T-SEC-35..51 (S-01..S-06, S-08..S-12; D-40a, #23).** Written from the report's findings and the updated SPEC items 3, 5, 8 and 13 only. Rename rows (T-SEC-35/36) drive core's `confirmRename` with `newName` and assert a refusal before anything changes (D-40a rules out revert-after). T-SEC-37 accepts either a refusal of the pattern at submission or bounded matching, because SPEC only requires that no pattern can stall the instance; the limits were the coordinator's < 1 s, later revised (note 61). T-SEC-39 counts a FormValidation error as a refusal of a typing-time GET. T-SEC-43 needs no capacity constant: it keeps creating requests with the delivery thread stalled until a drop is logged (limit 3000). T-SEC-44 reads SPEC 5's 'stored parameters' as: raw fields named like `buildWithParameters` (`NAME=value`) when the browser's `json` blob is absent, else the job defaults stored explicitly (coordinator ruling on S-10). T-SEC-46 includes a positive control, reading SPEC 3 (#23) as the requester may still change the designation after losing Item/Read. MockAuthorizationStrategy matches sids literally, so the case-variant rows grant both spellings. The T-03-23 ruling (404 for a non-visible user, new T-03-26 for a designated non-requester) is in commit 3811f40 and is not part of these rows.

61. **Stabilising T-SEC-37 and T-02-42/43 under parallel surefire forks (`forkCount=1C`).** Neither change removes an assertion. **T-SEC-37:** the < 1 s limit measured machine load as much as the defect (a bounded implementation took 1123 ms on a 14-core machine running 14 forks). The limit is now 10 s, and to keep the row failing on the defect the hostile name grew from 30 to 40 characters: `(a\|a)*\1b` against `a`xN costs about 2^N steps, security-08 S-03 measured ~53 s for N = 30, so an unbounded match on N = 40 takes about 1024 times that (hours), far past both the 10 s bound and the 60 s give-up. The CLI call now also runs on the worker thread with the same 60 s give-up, so the defect fails the row instead of hanging it. A baseline-relative bound was considered and rejected: the baseline is subject to the same load noise, and the exponential gap makes an absolute bound unambiguous. **T-02-42/43:** `RealJenkinsExtension` restarts the child Jenkins on the port its first boot was given (`--httpPort=<first port>`); that port is free between the two JVMs, and under parallel forks another process can take it, so the restart dies in "Failed to start Jetty". The rows now call `withPort(0)` before the restart step so the second boot gets a fresh ephemeral port; the restart still boots from the same JENKINS_HOME, which is the property the rows measure.

62. **#25 file-name uniqueness rows T-04-05..07.** The crafted collision follows the issue: an encoded name over 250 characters used to become its first 180 encoded characters, `-`, and the SHA-256 of the full name. The hash case is not stated in the issue; the first red run of T-04-05 showed the old version emits upper-case hex (its decode of the shortened victim name ended in `-A094D2CE...`), so the fixture uses upper case and the collision is exact on every file system. (With lower case it still collided on macOS, whose default file system is case-insensitive - worth knowing for the fix: the shortened form must not equal a plain encoding even case-insensitively.) T-04-07 writes the legacy file literally in that form; it passes on the old code by construction and exists to keep the fallback after the fix.

63. **#17 locale and zone rows T-04-08..11.** Each row asserts its premise (the locale formats non-ASCII digits; the default zone puts the instant on another date) and restores the default locale and zone afterwards. T-04-09 checks that no file of the deleted month survives under any digit form by mapping every digit of each file name through `Character.digit`. T-04-11 asserts both zone directions so a helper pinned to either one fails.

64. **Bounded page loads, rows T-10-08, T-12-06, T-12-08.** "Reads a bounded amount regardless of how many records a month holds" has no public counter, so it is measured as heap allocation (HotSpot `getTotalThreadAllocatedBytes`, JavaScript and CSS off in the client): the same screen over 35,000 records may allocate at most 24 MiB more than over 1,500, and must render in < 2 s. Month files are generated by writing JSONL directly; the line layout is never guessed, it is copied from a line the store wrote for a sentinel record (`StoreDataFixtures`). One unmeasured load precedes each measured one, so an implementation that keeps a per-month index and rebuilds it because the files appeared behind its back is not charged for the rebuild. The issue talks about 150,000-record months; the rows use 35,000 (the SPEC section 6 7-day volume) at the coordinator's instruction to keep the suite's memory modest. The page reads the 7-day window, which holds all 35,000. The data months are 2025-08/09, not the real current month, because a first version on 2026-09 let a screen that reads the real current month pass the small-month measurement over the large data. The change list is measured on `history/?kind=changes` (the filtered change-record view the history screen offers); `changes/` did not honour `from`/`to` in the first run.

65. **Span cap by records, row T-12-07.** A 41-month span holding two records must list both; a cap of 36 month files drops the old one. The absurd span from 1990 must still answer 200 within 2 s.

66. **SPEC section 6 measurement, row T-10-09.** Measured once per run with 5,000 runs a day for 7 days, dashboard and history 7-day view, median of three loads after a warm-up, printed as `[spec6-measurement]` with JDK and CPU count. The figure goes into docs/HOSTING-READINESS.md (release-manager).

67. **Closed requests, rows T-04-13/14.** T-04-13 asserts on the documented store paths plus the services' lists, with a request closed yesterday and an active grant as the negative twin. T-04-14 has no counting hook (SPEC exposes none, and test-author does not read src/main), so "does not scan closed requests" is measured as cost: the fastest of three expiry passes over 10,000 copied closed request files may cost at most 250 ms more than the fastest over none, and the same pass must still expire an overdue PENDING request. The copies are written behind the store's back; they are closed, so ignoring them is correct.

68. **Retention vs queue, row T-04-12.** The expired month is made slow to delete (24,000 per-record files; one refused POST and one build run first, so page rendering and first-build start-up are not measured). The submissions start only once sampled files are observed disappearing and others still exist, and the row fails with a fixture message if the deletion finished too early. The orderings asserted (refusal answered, build started, RunRecord listed, each before the retention work returns) are what a store lock held across the whole deletion breaks. The row assumes the deletion runs inside `RetentionPeriodicWork.doRun()`; an implementation that hands the whole deletion to another thread would need the row revisited, not loosened.
