69. **Regression row T-04-15, commit 08f342c (#25 migration).** A real defect found and fixed after notes 62-63 were written: saving the snapshot of a long-named job (V) deleted the file at V's *legacy* shortened path (the old `-` scheme, T-04-05..07's fixture shape) even though a live, unrelated job (W) whose *plain* full name equals that exact string keeps its real, current baseline snapshot there — so W's baseline was destroyed and its next configuration change wrote **no** CONFIGURE record at all, breaking SPEC item 9's core guarantee that every configuration change is recorded. This is a different failure mode from T-04-06 (which checks that CONFIGURE diffs never leak content between the two jobs): the crafted pair is the same (W = `attackerFor(VICTIM)`, reusing `PathCodecUniquenessTest`'s fixture), but the assertion here is about W's baseline surviving V's *save* (and V's later *deletion*), not about diff content. The row gives W a baseline and one ordinary change before V exists at all — a control proving the harness records normally on its own — then creates and saves V twice (the trigger described in the defect), then changes W's configuration again and asserts exactly one *new* CONFIGURE record with a non-empty diff against W's own previous value (not an empty or missing baseline). A closing guard deletes V and repeats the same check once more, so neither saving nor deleting V may disturb W's baseline or its recording.
70. **D-38 / #24: a run request needs Item/Build as the requester (rows T-05-07..13).** Written from the new SPEC item 5 line only. Every entry point the line names has its own refused row and its own guard: the job form (T-05-07/08), the incident rerun POST (T-05-09/10), `RunRequestService.create` (T-05-11) and `IncidentService.rerun` (T-05-12); T-05-13 pins that the check is made on the requested job, not "anywhere". The refused rows assert **403** exactly, as the SPEC line says. That sits next to SPEC item 2 (#31: the per-job action is absent, 404, "for a user who may not use it"); these rows read the newer, more specific line as winning for a user who holds Request and Item/Read and lacks only Item/Build. If the owner means 404 there, the SPEC line changes first and the rows follow. The rerun POST sends the approver under both `approvers` (D-37) and `approver` (the T-SEC-10 form), because no rerun success row pinned the field name before. The premise of every actor is asserted in `setUp` through the job ACL.

71. **#26: approval does not depend on the approver's job access (rows T-05-14..17).** A Discover-only approver (T-05-14, HTTP; T-05-16, service) and an approver with no Item permission at all (T-05-15) approve a request designating them: the approval answers success, the request reaches EXECUTED and the job runs exactly once (queue empty after a second wait, next build number 2). BatchControl/Approve is granted `everywhere()` so that "no permission on the job" means no `Item/*` permission, not a missing Approve on the item ACL. The negative twin T-05-17 shows the policy still decides: a listed, job-readable approver who is not designated is refused with 4xx and nothing runs.

72. **#19: a switch change is transactional (rows T-01-07..10).** The save is made to fail from outside by replacing the global configuration's standard file (`$JENKINS_HOME/<descriptor id>.xml`, ARCHITECTURE section 5) with a non-empty directory; the fault is proven independently of the plugin by writing through core's `XmlFile` and expecting `IOException`. `$JENKINS_HOME/batch-control/` stays writable, so a toggle record or a revocation that ran before the save would be visible. The rows drive the real global form (`configure` then submit), finding the switch checkbox by name (`_.<field>` or `<field>`, exactly one asserted). The failing rows require an error response (>= 400): a failed save that answers 200 would tell the administrator the switch moved when it did not. Each failing row has a guard without the fault that toggles, records (exactly one CONFIG_TOGGLE with the right old/new detail, attributed to admin) and applies the side effect (revocation of the open window, or the run block). P-15's trap (a) is handled by asserting the switch is on before turning it off.

73. **Existing fixtures updated for D-38 (no assertion changed).** Requesters that file run requests gained Item/Build, each marked with a `D-38 (#24)` comment: CsvWhitespaceFormulaExportTest, DiscoverOnlyRequestScreenTest (u1 everywhere; b on `secret-j` and `open-k` only, keeping b's Discover-only premise on `secret-j`), ExpiryAndCancelTest, HistoryWebTest, IncidentTest, MarkerReuseAuditTest (the requester only; the re-user and the no-history user do not file requests), OwnerScenarioApprovalTraceTest, OwnerScenarioApproverDelegationTest, OwnerScenarioRejectionTest, QueueBlockTest (T-06-08), RequestConcurrencyTest, RequestIntegrityTest, RestartRecoveryTest, RunRecordListenerTest, RunRequestServiceTest (both strategies), SecretParameterMaskingTest, XssEscapingTest, and SecurityRegressionTest T-SEC-08 (u1 on the job). SecurityRegressionTest T-SEC-10 (`vr`) also gained Item/Build so that its 403 keeps measuring the missing Item/Read rather than the new check. Left unchanged on purpose: requesters that never submit (ActionVisibilityTest, PermissionsTest, SecurityRegressionTest T-SEC-11/15, ApproverIdentitySecurityTest's u1, whose Build already comes with Item/Read).

74. **D-42: a direct switch setter never fails the caller (rows T-01-11/12, security-09 S-01).** The web form stays transactional (T-01-07..10), while a direct `setChangeControlEnabled` call (T-01-11) and a JCasC apply (T-01-12) against the unwritable file (the note 72 fault) must not throw, must leave the switch on in memory and must write the toggle record. T-01-12 first asserts that the export names the symbol `batchControl:`, so the apply cannot pass by configuring nothing. Both are P0 because a throw there would abort a boot-time JCasC apply, which stops Jenkins from starting. The SEVERE log line D-42 mentions is not asserted, because these rows check results, not log output.

75. **S-02: one switch leaves the other untouched (rows T-01-13/14, security-09 S-02).** Both switches start on, and this is checked after a reload from disk. Toggling one switch must leave the other unchanged in memory, add no CONFIG_TOGGLE for it, and keep its value on disk after `load()`. The rows run in both directions, so a shadowing bug in either setter shows up.


80. **D-43 reverses T-04-07 (security-10 S-01/S-07).** SPEC item 4 now says files in the pre-release shortened form are not read, because the plugin was never released and a file at that path can only be another item's plain encoding. T-04-07 therefore asserts the opposite of what it used to: the planted pre-release file is not used as a baseline and is left byte-identical where it is. A guard save shows the job still keeps its own current-form baseline, so "not used" cannot be satisfied by recording no diffs at all. T-04-15 (note 69) is unaffected. No row asserted a rename of non-ASCII month files; T-04-09 only checks that retention leaves no file of the deleted month behind.

81. **security-10 S-01 regression, T-04-16.** This is the orphaned-plant case: N's current-form snapshot is absent and the pre-release path holds attacker content that already carries the grant holder's entry. If that content were N's baseline, the entry would look unchanged and survive the D-35b guard, and a restart migration would make the plant N's baseline for good. The row asserts the D-35b outcome (entry removed, GRANT_VIOLATION), that no diff mentions the plant's marker, and that the file is not moved across a restart.

82. **security-10 S-02 regression, T-10-10.** 70 parameters exceed the 64-member limit the review found in the fast reader. Every value is asserted on both screens, so a reader that drops the run, or drops some members, fails.

83. **security-10 S-03 regressions, T-12-09/10.** The 50,000-record cap value comes from the review, not from SPEC; the data are placed so that job A's record is older than the newest 50,000 lines of its month. The reached-cap notice is measured differentially, because every history screen carries a CSV link anyway: the capped screen's text must mention CSV more often than an uncapped screen with the same filters. The CSV completeness half follows SPEC's "the CSV export, which is complete".

84. **security-10 S-09 regression, T-04-17.** "Past retention" for an EXECUTED request must count the build's start. The build is held in the queue by running with zero executors while the plugin clock sits three months back, and it is released after the clock is reset. The twin (built at the old time) is deleted, so a retention that keeps every EXECUTED request fails too.

85. **#21 TRIGGER_BLOCKED rows T-06-43..48.** Frozen contract: `ChangeType.TRIGGER_BLOCKED`, target = job full name, detail names the cause kind (`TIMER`, `UPSTREAM`, `REPLAY`) and the blocking switch. For Replay the switch is read as `approvalRequired`, the only setting that blocks it. Coalescing is measured on the plugin clock (`BatchClock.setForTest`), not waited for: "at most one record per hour" for one job and cause kind, so a refusal 61 minutes after the recorded one must write a second record. T-06-47 is the twin: kinds and jobs are not merged, and a timer run that passes writes nothing. The upstream refusal uses `scheduleBuild2` with an `UpstreamCause` of a finished build rather than a Pipeline `build` step, so the upstream run does not fail and nothing else is recorded. The history and CSV reads pass an explicit `from`/`to` because the clock is fixed in 2025.

86. **#21 job-page notice rows T-06-49/50.** The switch is recognised by name, `blockTimer` or its label `Block timer` (likewise upstream), case-insensitively. The viewer is a plain reader, who has no Configure link, so "how to clear it" is measured inside the notice: the element naming the switch, or one of its three nearest ancestors, must mention the configuration (`configur`). T-06-50 reads the switches back after turning run control off and shows the notice again after turning it on, so a notice that is never rendered fails it.

87. **#22 run-link rule T-06-51, settles note 49.** D-44: one rule for every screen. The viewer without Item/Read is the designated approver, who sees the request by P-09 and, with ViewHistory, the history, incident and dashboard screens (history screens are outside P-09, S-12). A build link is any anchor whose href contains `job/<name>/1/`. The four screens that must link the run for a reader are the request detail, the incident detail, the history runs screen and the dashboard; the incident list is asserted only in the plain-text direction because SPEC does not say it shows the run. T-SEC-26's comment about the link is now superseded by this row.

88. **#22 Changes month default T-12-11.** The Changes screen (`batch-control/changes/`) with no month chosen must show the plugin clock's month (item 4: month buckets and screen dates follow the plugin clock). Two CREATE records a month or more apart make both directions measurable: with the clock fixed in 2025-03 only that record is listed, with the clock reset only the real-month one.

89. **security-11 N-01/N-02 rows T-04-18..20.** The oversized record is a well-formed line whose job name (or target) alone is 1.1 MiB, written between two ordinary lines; lines are templated from what the store wrote. The CSV rows assert the export completes (200), keeps the ordinary rows, and carries nothing of the oversized one. The notice is measured against a twin month holding the same two ordinary lines and no oversized one; the pinned screen contract is only that the notice contains one of "skipped", "oversized", "too large" or "1 MiB" (case-insensitive). `RecordPage#getOversized()` (frozen) is the count behind the notice and is not called here.

90. **SPEC item 6a activation rows T-06a-01..41 (#15, D-39).** Frozen names (given by the coordinator): `model.ActivationRequest` (`getId`, `getJobFullName`, `getAction()` → `ActivationRequest.Action` ACTIVATE|HOLD, `getReason`, `getRequester`, `getApprovers()`, `getDecidedBy`, `getStatus()` → `RequestStatus`), `policy.ActivationService` (`get()`, `isActivated(Job)`, `create(Job, Action, String reason, List<String> approvers)`, `approve(id, comment)`, `reject(id, comment)`), `ChangeType.ACTIVATED`/`HELD`; URLs `GET job/<n>/batch-control/activation`, `POST job/<n>/batch-control/activation/submit` (`action`, `reason`, repeated `approvers`), `GET batch-control/activations/`, `POST batch-control/activations/<id>/approve|reject|cancel` (`comment`). Expected beyond the frozen names: `ActivationService.load(id)`, `ActivationService.list()` (all requests), and `ActivationService.getState(Job)` → `model.ActivationState` (`isActivated`, `getActivatedBy`, `getRequestId`, `getDeactivatedBy`) — the ARCHITECTURE record, needed because SPEC pins `activatedBy = upgrade`. Classes: ActivationGateTest (01..09), ActivationConfigPathTest (10..15), ActivationRequestTest (16..26), ActivationLifecycleTest (27..31), ActivationSeedingTest (32..37), ActivationScreenTest (38..41); shared helpers in ActivationFixtures.

91. **Reading of "run-controlled job", and the fixture rewrite of existing rows.** A run-controlled job is one with `approvalRequired=true` while run control is on, the set SPEC 6 and T-06-16 already call controlled; a job without it needs no activation (T-06a-07). Since a job created after the plugin's first start is not activated, and every JenkinsRule instance is such a first start, every existing row whose premise is "the timer/upstream door of a controlled job is open" would now fail for a reason it does not measure, and every row asserting that a job switch refuses a cause would pass for the wrong reason (non-activation). The rows were therefore rewritten, not relaxed: they activate the job through `BatchControlFixtures.activate(job, requester, approver)`, which files an ACTIVATE request and has it approved through `ActivationService` (the only path SPEC allows) and asserts the job is activated. Rows touched: T-06-05/06/07/10/11/12, the QueueBlockTest log-rate row, T-06-27/29, T-SEC-32, T-08-26/27 (the way out is now "switches off **and** an approved activation"), T-11-02/06, T-OS-04/06/08, T-06-43/44/46/47/48, T-RT-01 and the DiscoverOnlyRequestScreenTest fixture runs. T-12-04 moved its timer run to an uncontrolled job instead (note 97). NewJobActivationLockTest (T-08-30..36) and T-RT-05 are unchanged: their property assertions still pin D-34, and their refused-cause halves are now doubly guaranteed.

92. **Open points SPEC 6a leaves, asserted as read or not at all.** (a) *Superseded by D-45 (note 98):* T-06a-08 first pinned that a job created while run control was off stops on its timer once controlled; the ruling is the opposite and the row now asserts it. (b) *Superseded by note 101:* the TRIGGER_BLOCKED record for a missing activation now names `activation` as the switch. (c) Whether a pending ACTIVATE/HOLD request is invalidated by a rename or move of its job (the D-21 rule for run requests) is not specified; no row. (d) A HOLD of a job that is not activated, and an ACTIVATE of an already activated job, are not specified; no row.

93. **The REST and CLI config-path rows (T-06a-11/12/15) edit the job's own config.xml** and rely on the property's switches being serialised as `<blockTimer>`/`<blockUpstream>` elements (the SPEC field names). The fixture asserts both elements are present before editing, so a different serialisation fails the fixture loudly instead of letting the row pass on an unedited job; the premise (switches read back off) is asserted in every config-path row. T-06a-10 finds the checkboxes by a name ending in the field name (`_.blockTimer`).

94. **Store layout checked from outside (T-06a-16, T-06a-28..30, T-06a-32..35).** ARCHITECTURE "Activation store": `activation-requests/<id>.xml` is asserted by name; per-job state files are counted as `activations/*.xml` without a leading dot, one per job that has a state, so rename/move must relocate rather than copy and delete must remove; the encoded file name is not pinned. The `.schema` marker is asserted by name.

95. **Pre-upgrade home, black box (T-06a-32..37).** JenkinsSessionExtension: the first session's own boot sees no jobs (asserted: no ACTIVATED record), then creates the jobs with run control off (so no creation default applies), sets their properties as an operator of the previous release had them, and deletes `batch-control/activations/` — the one directory the previous release did not have. The next boot is therefore an upgrade of a home with existing jobs. T-06a-34 deletes the state files but keeps `.schema` to pin that seeding is keyed by the marker (DESIGN section 6: "not by 'is the directory empty'").

96. **Job-page screen contract (T-06a-38).** SPEC says the page "shows whether the job is activated or on hold and links to the activation request form"; the row recognises the state by wording, case-insensitively: "not activated" or "on hold" when not in service, "activated" without either phrase when in service, and the link by its target `batch-control/activation`. Both directions are asserted on the same job (never activated → activated → held) so an always-on notice cannot pass. If ui-dev chooses other wording, the contract is to be restated here, not the assertion widened.

97. **Monthly summary and activation requests (T-12-04).** SPEC 12's "요청 승인/반려 수" predates activation requests; whether an approved ACTIVATE counts is not specified, so T-12-04 no longer needs an activation: its second SUCCESS is a timer run of an uncontrolled job, which keeps every count unchanged. No row pins either answer.

98. **D-45 (T-06a-08 rewritten, T-06a-42 added).** A job created while run control is off is recorded as activated at creation (`activatedBy = uncontrolled`), so turning run control on or making the job approval-required later never stops its timer; T-06a-08 asserts the state, three timer runs across those changes, no activation request, and the AND guard (blockTimer on still refuses). T-06a-42 is the counterpart: created while run control is on, not activated until approved. The seeding rows' pre-upgrade session now creates its jobs with run control on, so creation cannot leave an ACTIVATED record behind and blur the one-record-per-job assertion (a premise asserts none exists); T-06a-35 turns run control on before creating its job.

99. **security-12 S-12-01 rows T-06-52/53.** The review's bound is 10,000 keys, but 10,001 jobs took more than four hours, so the row lowers the bound to 50 through the system property core-dev exposes (`...BlockedAttemptAudit.maxCoalescedKeys`) and uses 51 jobs, the smallest count that overflows it. The row pins the outcome only (one record per job, nothing thrown, nothing queued); whether an evicted key writes a second record on a later refusal in the same hour is allowed by the review ("eviction only ever adds a record") and not pinned. The failing write in T-06-53 is produced from outside by putting a directory where ARCHITECTURE section 5 says the month file is (`changes/YYYY-MM.jsonl`); the guard proves the failure path was exercised rather than skipped.

100. **D-46 contract reading (security-13).** While run control is on the unattended gate applies to every non-computed job whatever `approvalRequired` says (D-46 (a)), which supersedes note 91's "a job without approvalRequired needs no activation"; T-06a-07 is rewritten and raised to P0 (run blocking). Unattended means timer, upstream, SCM and any unclassified cause (D-46 (b)); the rows pin SCM and a custom `Cause` subclass (T-06a-45). *Settled by the D-46 amendment (note 109):* a cause-less queue submission or one with only `Cause.LegacyCodeCause` (what `JenkinsRule.buildAndAssertSuccess` and `scheduleBuild2(0)` produce) is unclassified and needs activation. Before that ruling, helper jobs that must simply run in the rows touched here are now started by a `UserIdCause` (`ActivationFixtures.userCause`), and T-06-26/35 start their first build that way; the remaining cause-less call sites are handled in note 109.

101. **TRIGGER_BLOCKED for a missing activation (T-06a-09, T-06a-43/44), supersedes note 92 (b).** The record's detail names the cause kind (`TIMER`, `UPSTREAM`) and `activation` as the blocking switch, matched case-insensitively (the caller's contract "switch=activation"). T-06a-43 counts exactly two records (one per kind) before the clock moves; the refusal at T0+31 min falls in the same coalescing hour and is not counted.

102. **Computed folders carry the activation (D-46 (c), T-06a-31, T-06a-46/47, T-10-07).** `ActivationService.isActivated`, `getState` and `create` take an `Item` so a `WorkflowMultiBranchProject` can be passed; `BatchControlFixtures.activate` and the `ActivationFixtures` form helpers take an `Item` for the same reason. T-06a-46 files the request through the multibranch project's own `job/<mb>/batch-control/activation/submit`, so the job-level action must exist on computed folders too. Branch indexing itself (`mb.scheduleBuild2(0)`) is assumed to stay schedulable while the multibranch project is not activated: indexing creates jobs, it does not run one; the indexing-triggered branch build is what must not run. Whether `isActivated(branch)` reports the ancestor's state is not pinned (T-06a-31 no longer asserts it).

103. **Existing rows changed by D-46.** Contract changes: T-06a-07 (rewritten, P1→P0), T-06a-31 (rewritten), T-08-28 (SCM now refused until activated, then admitted with the D-34 switches on; method renamed `t_08_28_lockedNewJobBuildsFromScmCauseOnceActivated`), T-06a-09 (the activation switch pinned, note 101), T-06a-08/32 (activatedBy values, note 104). Fixture-only changes, assertions unchanged: T-10-03 (the uncontrolled `cause-x` is activated after its USER run, its upstream Pipeline is uncontrolled and started by a human), T-10-07 (the multibranch project is activated first), T-11-01 (the uncontrolled cron job is activated first), T-12-04 (the second SUCCESS is a human run of an uncontrolled job, so the summary still sees no activation request, note 97), T-06-26/35 (first build by a human cause, note 100). No other row sends a timer, upstream, SCM or custom cause to a job created under run control without activating it first (checked across every test class that submits such causes).

104. **Non-user activatedBy values (S-13-10).** Seeded jobs carry `activatedBy = (upgrade)` and jobs (or computed folders) created while run control is off `(uncontrolled)`, values no user id can take. SPEC 6a and D-45 now spell them the same way.

105. **An undeletable state file, from outside (T-06a-49).** A regular file cannot be made undeletable portably without changing directory permissions, so the fixture replaces the job's one state file (`activations/*.xml`, the encoded name is read from the directory, note 94) with a non-empty directory of the same name. Whatever the implementation does with the failed delete, the observable contract is SPEC 6a's: the re-created job starts not activated.

106. **Invalidation of the other pending requests (T-06a-51).** The row assumes a second pending ACTIVATE for the same job can be filed (security-13's premise). SPEC says approving one request "invalidates" the others, so the status is pinned as `RequestStatus.INVALIDATED`, the status D-21 already uses for run requests. The HOLD is filed after the first approval, so which pending requests an approval invalidates beyond other ACTIVATE requests is not measured.

107. **The notification names the action (T-06a-52).** `Notification#getAction()` (new) returns `ACTIVATE` or `HOLD` for an activation request (null for other kinds); `kind` stays `ACTIVATION` as T-06a-41 pins. The ACTIVATE twin keeps a constant from passing.

108. **Not added as rows (security-13).** S-13-04's cache race (a HOLD or delete interleaved with an unlocked read) is not reproducible from outside without timing control; its observable guarantee is covered by T-06a-30 and T-06a-48. S-13-09's restored directory is approximated by T-06a-48 (a folder delete gives the job no delete event of its own). S-13-11 (seeding relies on running as SYSTEM) has no observable effect under JenkinsRule, whose initializers always run as SYSTEM. S-13-10's "always record an uncontrolled activation" is not pinned: SPEC 6a requires an ACTIVATED record for seeding only.

109. **Cause-less and LegacyCodeCause submissions are unattended (D-46 amended, SPEC 6a).** T-06a-53 pins the ruling itself. Every other test that builds a job created under run control through `buildAndAssertSuccess`, `buildAndAssertStatus` or `scheduleBuild2(0)`, and whose point is not activation, now activates the job first: `BatchControlFixtures.activateAsAdmin` (the administrator's self-approved ACTIVATE) on secured instances, `activate(job, requester, approver)` with the class's own ids on unsecured ones. 27 call sites in 15 classes: T-SEC-31 (CsvWhitespaceFormulaExportTest), T-04-08, T-10-10, T-04-12 (both the warm-up and the pooled submission, via one activation) and its expired-month fixture, T-SEC-10 (SecurityRegressionTest), the RequesterBuildPermissionTest incident helper, T-10-02, T-11-03 (two jobs), T-11-07, the IncidentTest `openIncident` helper, T-12-02, T-12-03 (two jobs), T-SEC-06 (incident endpoints), T-06-45 and the TriggerBlockedAuditTest upstream helper, T-06a-02's caller, T-RT-01's caller, T-06-28's upstream, T-06-24, T-06-04, T-06-05/10/11/12's upstream jobs. T-12-04's `sum-f`/`sum-u` became human runs of uncontrolled jobs instead (2 more sites, note 97: an approved ACTIVATE might be counted by the summary). Three sites found later, after the sweep above: T-RT-13's `leak-x` (IncidentTest) and T-SEC-19's `secret-direct` (SecretParameterMaskingTest) are the same cause-less `scheduleBuild2(0, (Cause) null, ...)` pattern, activated with `activateAsAdmin`; T-06-32's `btr-free` (PluginInteractionBuildTokenRootTest) is a build-token-root submission (`RemoteCause`), also unattended under D-46 (a), activated with `activate(job)` before the anonymous GET — its point (the token path works on a job the gate lets through) is unchanged, only the fixture's premise is. 30 call sites in 17 classes overall. Not changed, because the job was created while run control was off (activated at creation, D-45) or run control is off when it builds: the Job DSL seeds (T-08-23, T-08-36, the ChangeRecordTest seed), T-08-27/33's callers, T-01-09, ApprovalScreenRecentRunsTest, StoreDurabilityTest. Multibranch indexing (`mb.scheduleBuild2(0)`) is not a job build and stays as it is (note 102).

110. **D-47 contract reading (security-14 S-14-01 BLOCKER, SPEC 6a).** Whether a queue submission is human is decided by the submission's own cause, not by causes it inherits: an automatic retry (naginator and the like) copies the retried build's causes, but that does not make the retry itself human, so it needs activation on every job — approval-required or not — like any other unattended cause (D-46), and on an approval-required job it is separately refused as marker re-use (SPEC item 6, D-30) if it carries the consumed marker. A Rebuild click is different in kind: the click itself creates a fresh `UserIdCause` at the moment of the POST, so it is a person acting now and needs no activation on an uncontrolled job (T-06-26 unchanged, note 112). New rows: T-06-54 (the automatic-retry refusal and its TRIGGER_BLOCKED record) and T-06-55 (marker re-use recorded regardless of `approvalRequired`). Fixture-only change: T-06-35 (note 111).

111. **T-06-35 activates its job first (D-47).** Before D-47, T-06-35's uncontrolled job needed only a human first build (note 100) for the automatic retry to run; D-47 makes the retry itself an unattended cause, so the job must also be activated (`BatchControlFixtures.activate`) or the retry T-06-35 relies on would be refused. T-06-54 is the row for the same setup left not activated — its point is the refusal and the TRIGGER_BLOCKED record, following the T-06a-09 contract (note 101): detail names `activation` as the blocking switch, matched case-insensitively; the cause kind an automatic retry's TRIGGER_BLOCKED record names (if any) is not pinned, since the harness cannot know it without `src/main`.

112. **T-06-26 needs no activation (D-47).** The row is unchanged; only a comment was added citing D-47, because a Rebuild click's cause is a live `UserIdCause` created by that very POST, not one inherited from a retried build — the distinction D-47 draws is exactly between these two plugins' causes.

113. **T-06-55: marker re-use survives approvalRequired being turned off (D-47, P-12).** T-06-17..19 (note 37) predate the record's ratified shape and still assert only "a type of its own, not one of the eight SPEC-defined types"; T-06-55 is the first row to assert the ratified contract directly (P-12, DECISIONS.md): `ChangeType.MARKER_REUSE_BLOCKED`, `target` = the job the marker was presented on, `user` = the attempting account. Its premise is D-47's second sentence: the marker is bound to the request id, consumed once, and a re-use is recorded whether or not the job currently requires approval — so turning `approvalRequired` off after the marker was consumed must not let the same marker pass or go unrecorded. T-06-17..19 are left as they were; restating them against the ratified type name is not part of this change.

114. **S-14-02 (HIGH) not added as a row: not expressible with `MockAuthorizationStrategy`.** The finding's scenario — a viewer holding Item/Read on a computed folder's branch job but nothing on the multibranch project, still reaching the branch's own rendered page (where `JobActivationNoticeAction/jobMain.jelly` would disclose the ancestor's name/state) — cannot be built with the harness's authorization strategy. `MockAuthorizationStrategy` grants are per-item-path only (confirmed from its source: `onItems` matches exactly `item.getFullName()`, with no propagation to ancestors or descendants), and reaching a nested item's own page requires Jenkins core to resolve every ancestor segment of the URL first; this codebase's own T-SEC-20 premise (`DiscoverOnlyRequestScreenTest`) already establishes that core *throws* `AccessDeniedException` for an ancestor held with Discover only (not Read) — it does not fall through and let resolution continue to the child. So a viewer with zero grant (not even Discover) on the multibranch project gets 404 resolving it, and a viewer with Discover-only gets 403 — neither ever reaches the branch job's own page to exercise `jobMain.jelly` at all. The finding's own wording ("reachable with **folder-scoped strategies**") points at the same gap: such strategies (e.g. Role-based Authorization Strategy's pattern-matched job roles) are documented to imply Discover/Read on an item's ancestors specifically to keep it navigable, which is exactly the propagation `MockAuthorizationStrategy` does not do. **Request: security-reviewer confirm the finding was reproduced with a real folder-scoped strategy (which one) and, if so, whether the disclosure occurs on the branch's own page (`jobMain.jelly`, requiring ancestor Read to reach) or on some other, flatter surface (a listing screen, an inbox) that a viewer could reach without traversing the ancestor — the matrix needs the latter to write a row.** `ComputedFolderActivationAction/summary.jelly` has the same reachability property if it renders on the computed folder's own page: a viewer needs Read on the folder to see it at all. The fix itself ("show name and state only with Read on the carrier; otherwise a neutral notice") is not disputed — only how to reproduce the *unfixed* state from outside is.

115. **T-06a-54 (security-15 S-15-01): a cause's Java type alone does not make it human.** D-47 lists "an approved request's marker" and causes a live person creates now (Build Now, CLI, REST, a Rebuild click) as the human cases; a `Cause.UserIdCause` manufactured by code running as `ACL.SYSTEM2` is none of these — its `userId` is null because `User.current()` resolves against the authentication in effect when the cause is built, and SYSTEM2 names no user. The row schedules such a build directly (`Queue`/`scheduleBuild2` under `ACL.as2(ACL.SYSTEM2)`, mirroring T-06a-04's already-accepted refusal shape: null or a guidance `Failure`) against a job that is `approvalRequired=false` and not activated, so the only thing that could still stop it is the activation gate correctly refusing to treat the fake cause as human; the TRIGGER_BLOCKED record follows the T-06a-09 contract (note 101). The guard is T-06a-07/T-06a-04's own premise the other way up: a *real* logged-in user's Build Now on the identical, still non-activated job must still succeed, or the row would just be re-proving "non-activated jobs are locked" instead of pinning the SYSTEM-authored-cause misclassification S-15-01 found.

116. **Job-page manual-run notice (T-06-56..58, e2e-03 DEF-01).** The notice is recognised in `#main-panel` only (the side panel's "Request Run" is T-06-15's) as the smallest element whose text contains both "manual" and "approv", case-insensitively; "link to the request screen" is an `<a>` resolving to `job/<name>/batch-control/` (query and fragment ignored, trailing slash optional), not the activation form beneath it. The jobs are activated and have blockTimer/blockUpstream off so neither the activation notice (T-06a-38) nor the trigger-lock notice (T-06-49) can be mistaken for it. For a user without Batch Control permission the notice stays and no such link exists anywhere on the page (SPEC 2: the per-job action is absent for them). If ui-dev words the notice without "manual", the contract is to be restated here, not widened.

117. **Refusal page wording (T-06-59/60, e2e-03 DEF-02).** The parameters-form path is core's `build?delay=0sec` form submitted through `JenkinsRule#submit`. "Says whom to ask" is read as naming a configured approver (the fixture's only one, `a1`) or an administrator; the row accepts either. "No request URL" for nobc means neither an anchor into batch-control (plugin static resources excepted) nor a `/batch-control` URL in the visible text (the word "batch-control" alone may stay). T-06-13 (requester, POST build) is unchanged.

118. **Refused re-runs are recorded (T-06-61..65, e2e-03 DEF-03).** Every job is activated so the approval rule is the only reason left to refuse; a TRIGGER_BLOCKED `activation` record (T-06-54) cannot satisfy these rows by accident. Whether naginator or rebuild copies the approval marker onto its submission is not ours to know, so a re-run of an approved run accepts either type; a MARKER_REUSE_BLOCKED record must then name the consumed request id and, for a click by u1, `user` = u1 (D-30). A re-run of a manual run has no marker, so T-06-64 pins TRIGGER_BLOCKED. One refusal per job keeps the hourly coalescing out of the count. T-06-24/25/33/34 keep asserting the refusal; these rows add the record, as e2e-03 asked.

119. **No design references in operator wording (T-08-47, e2e-03 DEF-04).** The four GRANT_VIOLATION paths SPEC names (name restriction on create, on rename, a creation payload's authorization property, a grant-only Configure writing an authorization entry) are produced once each and every detail is checked against `\(D-|SPEC|\bD-\d+`; so is every line of `changes.csv`. The detail must stay non-blank, so deleting the text cannot pass. Job names in the fixture avoid those substrings.

120. **grantId for a Configure conferred by a Create grant (T-09-19/20, e2e-03 DEF-05).** SPEC 9 links `grantId` when the changer holds an active grant at change time; D-35c makes the CREATE grant the source of bob's Configure on the item it created, so that grant is the one linked. Both the REST and the form save are asserted (e2e-03 used the form). The negative twin uses c1's native Configure on the same item. `Grant#getId()` is compared with `ChangeRecord#getGrantId()` as ChangeRecordTest does.

121. **Activation form breadcrumb (T-UI-22, e2e-03 DEF-06).** No SPEC acceptance line covers breadcrumbs, hence a T-UI row. Breadcrumbs are read from core's `li.jenkins-breadcrumbs__list-item`; the row requires the crumb list to be present and to include the job, so a page rendering no crumbs cannot pass. "contains 'activat'" is the pinned wording.

122. **The standing-permission monitor names the holders (T-08-48, e2e-03 DEF-07).** SPEC 8 says the monitor warns when a user holds Item/Configure, Create or Delete without a grant; e2e-03 found it names nobody. The row reads the rendered `manage/` page as the administrator (the first screen-level assertion on this monitor; T-08-06 stays at `isActivated()`), with a build authenticator configured so the strategy monitor stays quiet, and requires the user and the group sid to appear, a reader not to, and no "is not a Batch Control strategy" hedge while the variant is installed.

123. **Invalid global settings (T-CFG-04, e2e-03 DEF-08).** "A message next to the field" is read as Jenkins' field validation: the global form's check method of each field (`descriptorByName/io.jenkins.plugins.batchcontrol.config.BatchControlGlobalConfiguration/check<Field>`, POST with the crumb as the form does) answers an error for the invalid value and none for a valid twin. The pinned method names are `checkMaxGrantMinutes`, `checkPendingTimeoutHours`, `checkApprovedRunTimeoutMinutes`, `checkRetentionMonths`, `checkGrantDurationOptions`, `checkIncidentResults`; the form fields are found by name (`<field>`, `_.<field>` or `*.<field>`). The submitted form must not be answered as a success (core's success is the redirect to `manage/`), must carry no crash text, and must store nothing invalid; T-CFG-03 (setter) stays.

124. **Configuration and strategy screens (T-02-44 DEF-10, T-02-46 DEF-20).** SPEC 6 usability: "The BatchControl/Manage permission is enough to open and save the Batch Control configuration." A Manage holder without Overall/Manage cannot reach core's `manage/configure`, so the row finds the page from the Batch Control screen by a link captioned "configur…" or "settings" (outside `/job/`) and requires a form with `pendingTimeoutHours`; the URL is not pinned. T-02-46: role-strategy's Assign Roles and Manage Roles pages call `checkName`, `checkPattern` and `checkForWhitespace` on the installed strategy's descriptor; the row calls them on the variant at both `descriptor/` and `descriptorByName/` and on role-strategy's own descriptor as the control. The rendered cells are only observable in a browser (role-strategy's table.js does not run in HtmlUnit, see T-02-13).

125. **Refused forms keep the input (T-05-18, DEF-09).** The run request goes through the real form (the parameter is found by its pre-filled default); the reject form through the request screen; the grant request by POST, because the grant form's duration control is not pinned. "Not a bare error page" means the title does not start with "Error"; "message next to the field" is read as the answer naming the field (reason, comment, the limit). If the browser-side `required` attribute stops the submission, HtmlUnit returns the unchanged form, which satisfies the row the same way a browser's native message does.

126. **No dead links on the dashboard and history (T-02-45, DEF-11).** `UsabilityFixtures.assertNoDeadLinks` opens every same-instance anchor of `#main-panel` as the viewer and requires < 400. a3 (Approve, not designated, no Item/Read) is the e2e-03 case; a2 (designated) and u1 are covered by the same check; u1 is also the control that the request link is offered at all.

127. **Deleted builds (T-06-69, DEF-23).** The job is uncontrolled and was created while run control was off (activated at creation, D-45); run control is turned on before the builds so that they are recorded (SPEC 9). T-06-51's link rule is unchanged; this row adds the deleted-build case of the same rule.

128. **CLI refusal (T-06-66, DEF-14).** CLI output is stdout + stderr of `CLICommandInvoker`. T-06-14 keeps asserting the guidance; this row forbids the "Unexpected exception" frame, stack frames and the exception class name.

129. **Trigger-lock notice without approvalRequired (T-06-67, DEF-15).** Same recognition as T-06-49 (switch name or label, "configur"), read in `#main-panel`; jobs are activated so the activation notice cannot be the only one. T-06-49/50 keep their approvalRequired=true fixtures.

130. **Replay (T-06-68, DEF-16).** The POST mimics core's form submission of `replay/run` (`mainScript` plus the `json` form payload). Whether the refusal is 4xx or a redirect to an explanation is not pinned; its text must name approval and carry none of the crash texts. T-06-45 (the record) is unchanged. **Coordinator ruling (e2e-03 part 2):** workflow-cps contributes the Replay link and another plugin cannot hide it, so for this control we do not own the usability line reads: the link may stay visible; a click on an approval-required job is refused with a plain-words HTML page explaining why and linking the request form for users who may open it (the administrator here), and the job page carries the approval notice (T-06-56). The earlier "Replay not offered" assertion is withdrawn by that ruling; see docs/LIMITATIONS.md items 40/41 for the same situation with other plugins' links.

131. **Never-succeeding build entries (T-06-70, DEF-25) and the T-UI-16/18 revision.** SPEC 6 says "Build Now" is replaced by "Request Run", and the usability line says a control is shown only to users who can use it. On an approval-required job every direct build is refused, for the administrator too, so rebuild's Rebuild / Rebuild Last are not offered. **Coordinator ruling (e2e-03 part 2) on core's build entry:** core draws the Build Now link for every Item/Build holder and a plugin cannot remove it, so T-UI-16/18 keep their original contract (one "Request Run" and the renamed "Direct Build (needs approval)" entry at core's build URL) and now also require that a click on it is refused with a plain-words explanation naming approval (docs/LIMITATIONS.md); T-06-70 no longer forbids that entry. **Coordinator ruling (e2e-03 part 2):** naginator's Retry link cannot be hidden by another plugin (docs/LIMITATIONS.md item 41), so it may stay visible; the row instead requires its click to be refused with a plain-words HTML page naming approval and linking the request form for the requester (no "Oops!", no generic toast), nothing queued, and the approval notice on the job page. Nothing else in the row is loosened. T-UI-17 is unchanged; note 47's "core's entry cannot be hidden" stands (confirmed by the ruling).

132. **Trigger-switch help (T-06a-55, DEF-21).** The help is read through `descriptorByName/<BatchControlJobProperty descriptor id>/help/<field>` (core's `Descriptor#doHelp`, the URL the configure page's help button opens), so the file name is not pinned. "Mentions activation" is `activat`; the forbidden sentence is the one e2e-03 quoted.

133. **Request entries without Item/Build (T-05-19, DEF-12).** D-38 refuses every run request without Item/Build, so the entries are hidden, not refused. The job's request screen may still exist for the activation form (SPEC 6a needs only Request + Item/Read), so the row forbids the run request form (`batch-control/submit`), not the screen. T-05-07/09 (the 403) are unchanged.

134. **Cancellation attribution (T-07-08, DEF-13).** SPEC 3 names `decidedBy`/`decidedAt` for the closing decision; a cancellation closes the request, so the row pins them to the canceller, on the model, the request screen and `requests.csv` (T-13-18's column). The user id `mgr7` is chosen so that it cannot appear on the screen by accident.

135. **Invalidation reason (T-07-09, DEF-17).** The screen must say renamed / moved and name the new full name; the exact wording is not pinned. The untouched request is the negative twin.

136. **Monthly summary screen (T-12-12, DEF-22).** T-12-04 pins the JSON counts of `history/summary?month=` (note 23). A person reaches the summary through the history screen's link (captioned "summary", or pointing at `history/summary`); that target must be HTML with word labels. The link today points at the JSON endpoint (observed when the row was written), so ui-dev gives the screen its own URL (or content negotiation) and keeps T-12-04's contract.

137. **Expired windows and re-request (T-08-49, DEF-18).** D-33 moved the expiry guidance to `batch-control/grants/`: remaining time of active windows, history of expired windows, a re-request link. Time is moved with `BatchClock` and `ExpiryPeriodicWork.doRun()` is called (notes 1, 2). "Shown as expired" is the smallest main-panel element naming the scope and the word "expired"; the re-request link is captioned "again", "re-request" or "renew" and must open the grant request form (`grants/create`). Pre-filling the scope is not required.

138. **Name restriction while typing (T-08-50, DEF-19).** T-SEC-39 accepts a 4xx or a validation error for a typing-time check; this row narrows it to a 200 validation answer naming the pattern (`app-[0-9]+`), which also satisfies T-SEC-39. The log check records the root logger at INFO and rejects any record with an `AccessDeniedException` (or its name) while typing. The refused POSTs must name the pattern instead of core's "missing the Job/Create permission".

139. **Delete veto advice, refused grant request record, grant mail content (T-08-51 DEF-26, T-08-52 DEF-27, T-13-20 DEF-24).** T-08-51: the veto for a user without RequestGrant must not send them to the Grants screen (403 for them, SPEC 2) and must say whom to ask ("administrator" or "ask"); the RequestGrant holder is the control. T-08-52: the usability line's "recorded history names who did what" and README's "both refusals explain themselves and are recorded"; the record type is not pinned (one new record by the requester whose detail or target names change control). T-13-20: D-36 lists id, scope, requester, link and reason; D-40 says the restriction "is shown to the approver", which the row applies to the mail. e2e-03 routes the D-36 wording to a DECISIONS proposal; the row is P2 and may be rephrased when that decision lands.

140. **No back link on refusal pages (T-UI-23, e2e-run3 DEF-28).** The coordinator's contract: Batch Control screens carry no hierarchical "Back to ..." link, since core's breadcrumb already leads back; A-11 found one only on the gate's new "Approval required" page. The row reads the three refusal answers a person meets and forbids the text "back to" and any anchor whose caption starts with "Back". Each answer must still name approval, so an empty page cannot pass.

141. **Group holders in the standing-permission monitor (T-08-53, e2e-run3 DEF-29).** T-08-48 names a user and a group; A-03 found that a group (`authenticated`) is expanded to every known account and shown as users. The row requires one line that names the group with Configure and says "group", and forbids the member accounts (which have no Configure entry) anywhere on the page. Re-checked against SPEC 8 at the coordinator's request: the warning is about holders of Configure "from the authorization strategy"; an account whose Configure comes only from the `authenticated` group is covered by the group entry and is not a user entry (`member29d` has no entry of its own at all, `member29a..c` only Read entries). The internal SYSTEM identity is not a strategy holder either (A-03 also showed "User SYSTEM: Job/Configure"). The exact wording ("Group authenticated: Job/Configure") is not pinned.

142. **Re-serialised internal field (T-09-21, e2e-run3 DEF-30).** throttle-concurrents adds `<configVersion>` when the posted XML is re-serialised; that is not user-editable configuration (SPEC 9). The fixture removes the element from the stored file and reloads, as T-09-13 does for version attributes, so the POST reproduces the A-12 diff. The description edit is the in-row guard.

143. **Build-page notice (T-06-71, e2e-run3 DEF-31/32).** Rebuild and Retry live on build pages, and their click ends in a generic "Failed."; the SPEC 6 notice sentence therefore reaches build pages too. The recognition and link rules are those of T-06-56/57. DEF-32 (a manual Retry merged into the automatic retry's SYSTEM record) is not given a separate row here, as the coordinator grouped it with DEF-31.

144. **build-token-root refusal body (T-06-72, e2e-run3 DEF-33).** A script caller only sees the response, so the refusal must carry a `text/plain` body naming an approved run request. The status stays any non-2xx (403 is fine).

145. **Core token trigger (T-06-73, e2e-run3 DEF-34).** Core answers a scheduled token build with 302 to the job page, so a refused one must not use any 3xx. The caller is an authenticated requester because core refuses the anonymous call before the gate (B5-06). P0: a script that reads the refusal as a started run is a run-blocking visibility defect.

146. **Manual Retry attribution (T-06-74, e2e-run3 DEF-32).** PR-05 found u1's refused Retry merged into the SYSTEM record the automatic retry had written 25 s earlier, so the history did not say that u1 tried a re-run. SPEC 6 records every refused retry, and the usability line wants recorded history to name who did what; the per-hour coalescing of SPEC 6 (#21) applies to one job and cause kind, and it must not erase the actor. The automatic retry stays SYSTEM (D-47: unattended). The record type follows T-06-61/62 (either type accepted); a TRIGGER_BLOCKED record must name the retry.

147. **Self-grant guard feedback (T-02-47..49, e2e-03 DEF-35, D-48).** The SPEC line under D-35b lists the form, `config.xml`, `createItem` and a copy; the form and the POST are pinned here, and the restore itself stays with T-02-22. The job carries a Read entry for bob so that matrix-auth's form offers a row in which bob can tick Configure without the JavaScript add-user dialog. The message is checked by fragments, not by wording: the item's full name, "authoriz", a not-kept phrase (not kept / reverted / removed / restored / discarded), "grant" or "temporar", "other" and "saved", "administrator". T-02-49 is the "nothing changes for saves the guard does not touch" twin.

148. **Activation breadcrumbs (T-UI-24, e2e-03 DEF-06).** T-UI-22 pins the last crumb. A-11 in e2e-run3 found the middle crumb still captioned "Request Run" and opening the run request form. This row forbids any crumb link to `job/<name>/batch-control` (the form) and any "Request Run" caption, and requires the job crumb so that a page without crumbs cannot pass.

149. **Role-strategy group holder (T-08-56, security-18 S-18-01).** The report accepts a fallback that names the member instead; the coordinator asked that the group be listed, as in T-08-53, so the row pins the group.

150. **Non-numeric configVersion (T-09-22, security-18 S-18-02).** A config.xml POST is re-serialised by core, so content that no field backs never reaches the stored file (T-SEC-33, T-09-21). The row therefore uses a test JobProperty whose own fields are named `configVersion`, standing in for a plugin where such a field is meaningful configuration. T-09-21 is the numeric twin.

151. **Retry of a remote-started build (T-06-75, security-18 S-18-03).** naginator copies the retried build's causes, so the RemoteCause of the token-started build rides along. The click is a person's retry (T-06-74), so the row asks for the HTML refusal, a TRIGGER_BLOCKED record by u1 naming the retry, and no REMOTE_RUN_BLOCKED record. The enum constant is looked up by name, because no test before this row referenced it.

152. **No notice action while run control is off (T-01-15, security-18 S-18-06).** SPEC 1 and CLAUDE.md say that existing Jenkins behaviour does not change while the switch is off; a build's `api/json` is public surface. CauseAction is required so that an empty or unparsed array cannot pass. api/json renders an action that exports nothing as an anonymous `{}` (verified: the actions array reads the same with run control on and off), so the class check is also made on `getAllActions()`, the public API that api/json is built from.

153. **D-48 ruling on creation and copy (T-02-35, T-08-47).** Main session ruling (2026-09-30), from D-48 and the SPEC line under D-35b: whenever the guard strips authorization entries, the save answers 403 with the D-48 message. This includes `createItem` with an authorization payload and a copy whose property is stripped. Both rows used to expect an answer below 400. They now assert 403 and the same message fragments as T-02-47/48, through the shared `GrantSelfGrantFeedbackTest.assertGuardFeedback`. The item may be named by its full name or its full display name. Their existence, removal and record checks are unchanged. T-02-34 asserts no status for its payload POST and is left as it is.


154. **Retry cause without a person on a request thread (T-06-76, security-19 S-19-01).** The report says no shipped plugin produces this submission today (naginator always adds the clicking user's UserIdCause). A test root action makes it reachable through public API: `Queue.schedule2` with a CauseAction, called inside u1's POST. The outcome pinned is the refusal, which is what the same causes get on a non-request thread (T-06-34). The report's REMOTE_RUN_BLOCKED record is not pinned, because SPEC 6 lets the record type follow the refusal path.

155. **Attribute on a numeric configVersion leaf (T-09-23, security-19 S-19-02).** Core drops an attribute that no field backs when it re-serialises a posted config.xml, so the attribute is backed by a test field that XStream writes with `ToAttributedValueConverter` (the annotation is processed on `Items.XSTREAM2` in the row). T-09-21 is the bare numeric twin that stays silent.

156. **No response wrapper while change control is off (T-01-16, security-19 S-19-03).** The report gives no externally visible difference while the wrapper only forwards, so the row observes the wrapper itself, through the public servlet API: a test endpoint walks `ServletResponseWrapper#getResponse` and reports the classes. The premise with change control on makes sure that the walk can see the plugin's wrapper at all. The row does not pin which class wraps, only its package.


157. **The POST writer while change control is on (T-02-50, security-20 S-20-02).** D-48 says nothing changes for saves the guard does not touch, and CLAUDE.md says the same for other behaviour. The erroring writer stands in for a client disconnect. Whether writes start failing after the client closes its socket depends on socket buffer sizes and timing, so a disconnect is not a reliable observation in JenkinsRule. The erroring response is installed from a CrumbExclusion, because core's CSRF filter runs before the plugin filters; the row asserts from the wrapper chain that the plugin's wrapper really sits above it. The report's second point (an extra copy of a large String) is a memory effect with no visible output difference, so the row pins only that such a body arrives complete and unchanged.


158. **Retry clicked in the browser (T-06-77, e2e-03 DEF-32).** T-06-74 POSTs `retry/` with a crumb in the query. This row drives the page as a browser does: naginator's task link is a POST link, and core's JavaScript sends it with the crumb header. A recording WebConnection asserts that exactly one such POST left the page, so a click that sent nothing cannot pass or fail the row.

159. **Exact-name restriction on rename and CLI (T-08-57/58, e2e-03 DEF-36).** T-08-50 covers a regex restriction. These rows use the exact name `nightly.report` and a CREATE-only grant, as in the e2e run. The restriction is recognised by the name plus one of "restrict", "only", "allowed", "permitted", "match"; the wording is not pinned. The trailing-space name must be refused like any other non-matching name.

160. **Jobs whose builds run as SYSTEM (T-08-59/60, D-50).** "SYSTEM" together with the job name on Manage Jenkins, and "SYSTEM" on the request's detail page, are the recognised warning; the wording is not pinned. `own-job` (its own strategy) and the global-default twin are the negative cases, so a warning shown for every CONFIGURE request cannot pass. authorize-project is already a test dependency (pom.xml).


161. **Per-attempt records for a person's re-run (T-06-78, D-51).** SPEC 6 now keeps the hourly coalescing for unattended submissions only. All clicks happen within seconds, which is inside both the one-hour and the one-minute window, so the row needs no clock: the repeated click on #1 must merge (the one-minute rule), the click on #2 must not (a different attempt). A build is recognised as named by `#<n>` or `/<n>` in the record's target or detail; the wording is not pinned. The unattended half uses naginator's automatic retry on a second job, so its SYSTEM record cannot be confused with u1's.


162. **Budget and coverage of per-attempt records (T-06-79..81, D-51a).** All attempts fall within seconds, inside the rolling 10-minute window, so no clock is needed. T-06-79 counts every record by u1 written after its fixture (activation records excluded by id), whatever the type, because the summary record's type is not pinned; the summary is the one record new with the 21st refusal, and it must say that the further refusals are counted ("count"). D-51a as amended in 3715223 keeps records append-only: the summary is not rewritten, and one closing record at the end of the window gives the count and the builds. That closing record is not pinned. SPEC offers no public trigger for the end of the window, and the window is ten minutes of wall time: whether moving `BatchClock` ends it, or whether the record is written at the next event or by a periodic task, is not specified. Build naming follows note 161 (`#<n>` or `/<n>`), and the other build numbers must not be named. Replay needs Run/Replay, so the administrator is the person in T-06-80.

163. **D-50a revision (T-08-59/60, new T-08-61/62).** D-50a withdrew the per-job list of D-50: the monitor shows one fixed warning without job names, and the detail page shows it to users who may decide the request or hold BatchControl/Manage, never to the requester, without a hidden-job count. T-08-59 now checks the approver, a Manage holder and the requester; T-08-61 (a global default that follows the triggering user) and T-08-62 (a job's own build authorization) are the two S-21-01 cases where a warning is still due. "SYSTEM" remains the recognised warning; note 160 applies otherwise.


164. **Build authenticator fixture under D-50a.** `StrategyFixtures.configureBuildAuthenticator()` used to add an empty `MockQueueItemAuthenticator`, which maps no item and so leaves every build as SYSTEM; under D-50a the batch-control-strategy monitor is then rightly active, which masked what T-08-08 and the other strategy-monitor rows measure. The fixture now installs Authorize Project's global default running builds as `admin` (`GlobalQueueItemAuthenticator` + `SpecificUsersAuthorizationStrategy`), a configuration under which every build has a user identity. The assertions of the rows are unchanged.


165. **security-22 rows (T-08-60 revised, T-08-63/64, T-06-82/83).** D-50b counts a build identity with Overall/Administer or root-level Configure (without a grant) as unsafe, so T-08-60's global default now runs builds as a Read/Build-only account, the administrator case is T-08-63 and the root Configure case is T-08-64. The fixture `StrategyFixtures.configureBuildAuthenticator()` (note 164) now runs builds as `batch-builder`, an account without entries, for the same reason. T-06-82/83 build the chain while the job is uncontrolled and activated, then make it approval-required; the kind is read from the record's detail ("rebuild" / "retry"), the build as in note 161. Not added: S-22-02 (an authenticator that decides by the calling identity) is a documented limitation under D-50b, not a contract; S-22-03 (the closing count record surviving a restart) cannot be observed without the end of the D-51a window, which has no public trigger (note 162); S-22-04 (constant time, records appended under the queue lock) has no observable result on the public surface.


166. **Closing record at a clean shutdown (T-06-84, security-23 S-23-08).** `JenkinsSessionExtension` stops Jenkins cleanly between sessions, which is the case LIMITATIONS describes; a crash is not simulated. The count follows the ruling recorded in D-51a and SPEC line 107 (1a80eff): the closing record counts every refusal beyond the 20 per-attempt records, including the one that opened the summary, so 24 attempts give 4 and the record names #21..#24 (and none of #1..#20, which have their own records). Not added: S-23-03 (a failed store write during the closing flush) needs a store that fails once on append; the store is `FileStore.get()` with no public replacement seam, and making its files unwritable on disk would fail every write and is not portable, so the row would not measure the retry.


167. **Configuration audit and approver ids (T-CFG-05..08, T-UI-25; D-52, D-53, e2e-04 FD-01..03).** The realm is Jenkins' own user database, because the harness's dummy realm resolves every name and D-53 is about ids the realm does not resolve. The record types CONFIG_CHANGE and STRATEGY_CHANGE are looked up by name, since they are new with D-52. The configuration is driven on `batch-control-configuration/` (e2e-04 S1-02); the approver field is the text field whose name contains "approver". The Revert control is recognised by its caption or by a link or form to `batch-control-strategy/revert`.

168. **Requests that end without a decision (T-13-21..23, T-05-20, T-07-10; D-54, D-55, FD-04..06).** Mail through mock-javamail as in EmailNotificationTest. A mail is recognised by the request id together with a word for the event in its subject or body; the REQUEST_CREATED mail is awaited and the mailboxes emptied first, so a later match is the event's. T-07-10 was already green on 8cc773c: both expired requests name their reason and the reasons differ.

169. **Refusal after a hold (T-06-85, FD-07).** The per-hour coalescing of SPEC 6 (#21) stays, but a change of the activation state in between must not merge a later refusal into a record written before it. The row asserts only that there are two records; the wording and whether a restart also opens a new record are not pinned.

170. **History user and dates (T-12-13/14, FD-09, FD-11).** The run's user is read as the word `u1` in its History row and runs.csv line. The date message is read in the text of the form that holds the `from` field; the wording is not pinned beyond the listed words.

171. **Grant form placement (T-UI-26, FD-08).** A browser's scroll position cannot be observed in HtmlUnit; document order in `#main-panel` stands for it: the form (action ending in `grants/create`) and the refusal's message must come before the first table (the lists). Whether the lists are paginated is not pinned.


172. **Store failure and the shutdown flush (T-06-86/87, S-23-03, S-23-06).** core-dev's test seam (security-24 S-24-04) makes these observable: `BlockedAttemptAudit.swapStoreForTesting(Store)` (restored after each row), `BlockedAttemptAudit.flushAtShutdown()` and `BlockedAttemptAudit.get().flushPersonSummaries()`. The failing store is a dynamic proxy over `Store` that forwards every call and throws once from `appendChangeRecord`, so the rows depend on no other method of the interface. The row checks that the failure was actually met, so a flush that never tried to write cannot pass. The end of the 10-minute window is reached by moving `BatchClock` (it drives the budget, as the premise of T-06-86 shows). Both rows were green on 99c402e. This supersedes the "not added" entry for S-23-03 in note 166.


173. **security-25 rows (T-CFG-09, T-10-11).** T-CFG-09 uses the realm of ConfigAuditTest, so `no-such-user` is really unknown, and the manager's POST is the premise that the check exists at that URL. T-10-11 drives a real Rebuild (the rebuild plugin copies the approved cause) instead of the report's synthetic cause list. The run's user and classification are read from the History row (`#n` prefix) and from runs.csv; the request id is the one `requestAndApprove` returns.


174. **security-26 rows (T-06-88, T-CFG-10..12).** T-06-88 uses the coordinator's form of S-26-01: an approval-required job whose timer is allowed, with the timer build waiting because there are no executors. The report's form (a job without approvalRequired and a user's build) exercises the same fold. The queue is read through the public `Queue.getItems()`. T-CFG-10 uses existing accounts, so the refusal can only come from the cap. T-CFG-11 reads "not checked" from the refusal (the report's wording); the realm's call count is not observed. T-CFG-12 uses a test subclass of the user database whose lookup throws `UserMayOrMayNotExistException2` for one id. Not added: S-25-04 (`swapStoreForTesting` refuses outside unit tests), because a test always runs as a unit test (coordinator ruling); S-25-03 and S-25-06 were not requested. T-CFG-10..12 were green on e924f2d; T-06-88 is red.


175. **Authorization entries for grant holders (T-02-51..56, D-58, DEF-38).** The script is changed by bob himself through `POST config.xml` inside his window (the D-35b guard lets that save through: it does not touch the authorization property), and the `properties` step writes the entry during the build. Builds run as an account without Configure, so the rows show that D-50a alone does not stop it. matrix-auth 3.3's `authorizationMatrix(entries: [user(...)/group(...)])` syntax is used; T-02-54's green run shows that the step does write an entry. "Revoked" in the request is realised as the end of the window (`BatchClock`); the result, no Configure for bob, is the same. The administrator's deliberate entry (T-02-55) goes through `POST config.xml`, which is an HTTP request as D-58's exception requires; the matrix-auth form was not driven. T-02-51..53 are red on a177462; T-02-54..56 are green.


176. **D-58a rework (T-02-51..65).** D-58a guards per item instead of per principal: an item in the scope of an active grant, or changed under a grant and not reviewed since (an HTTP save by a native Item/Configure or Overall/Administer holder), is guarded, and any widening on it is reverted whoever makes it, except an administrator's HTTP save. T-02-54 is inverted: an entry for a user who never held a grant is now reverted. T-02-52 is guarded because the configuration was changed under the grant, not because of the 30 days of D-58. The saves in T-02-58..60 are made by a script (`updateByXml` as SYSTEM), so no HTTP-save rule (D-35b, D-48) can be what reverts them. T-02-59/60 set the nonInheriting baseline before the grant and keep Job/Read for bob, a1 and c1, so that the grant can be filed and approved. The rename is made by the administrator programmatically. The review in T-02-64 is c1's description change through `POST config.xml`. On c06ee14: T-02-51/52/53/55/56/65 green; T-02-54, 57..64 red.


177. **D-58a (5) and alerts 31-33 (T-02-23 revised, T-02-66..68, T-SEC-52).** D-58a (5) removes the exemption of a native non-administrator Configure holder on a guarded item, so T-02-23 now expects 403 with the D-48 message and a GRANT_VIOLATION for c1. T-02-66 shows that such a reverted save is not the review. T-02-67 uses a creation inside the guarded folder and a build after the window, so only the state (not the active scope) can guard the item. T-02-68 restarts between the change and the build. Not added: retention ending the state. Retention can be run (`RetentionPeriodicWork.doRun()`, as T-04-13 does), but the state lives in the grant file's `changedItems`, and whether a grant becomes old enough for retention depends on month cut-offs that the row would have to fake for the grant file as well. Left for a later row. All of these were green on 286c122 (run with `-Daccess-modifier-checker.skip=true`: the branch's main code currently fails the access-modifier check at `BatchControlRoleBasedAuthorizationStrategy$DescriptorImpl:213`, `RoleBasedAuthorizationStrategy.SYSTEM_READ_AND_SOME_ROLES_ADMIN`).


178. **D-58b rows (T-02-64 reworked, T-02-64b, T-02-69..73).** D-58b (3) makes the review explicit: T-02-64 now reviews through `<job>/batch-control/markReviewed` (c1) and T-02-64b through the monitor (admin), each with a GUARD_REVIEWED record naming the reviewer (the type is looked up by name). T-02-70 is the old T-02-64 path (an ordinary save), now expected not to clear the state. T-02-71 replays with the form POST of T-06-68. T-02-72 uses nested folders (a multibranch branch job is the other descendant form and is not added). T-02-73 starts from a nonInheriting baseline so that "one merged property" is observable (with no baseline the correct revert leaves none). The first run answered 404 on the job-level URL; the coordinator ruled that the job's Batch Control page exists only for BatchControl/Request holders (LIMITATIONS 35), so T-02-64/69 give c1 and bob that permission and T-02-74 pins the 404 without it.


179. **Runs replayed under a grant (T-02-75..78, D-58c).** Pipeline Rebuild is workflow-cps's `replay/rebuild`; Replay is the form POST of T-06-68. The refusal is recognised as a plain refusal naming "temporary", "permission window" or "replayed under"; the record type of the refusal is not pinned (a new record by c1 on the job). Restart from Stage and the rebuild-plugin Rebuild are not added.


180. **security-30 rows (T-02-79/80).** T-02-79 needs the real pipeline-model-definition plugin for the declarative script and the Restart from Stage action (`<run>/restart/restart`); it is not a test dependency yet (Request to release-manager for pom.xml), so the row fails at its declarative fixture build until it is added. T-02-80 checks that a re-run of a marked run inherits the marker through the administrator's Pipeline Rebuild.


181. **e2e-03 final check rows (T-02-81..83, DEF-39..41).** Text rows: "Mark as reviewed" is the action's caption (D-58b); "approvalRequired" and "only from" are the misleading phrases the e2e run found. The Rebuild link is recognised by its href (`<n>/rebuild`), with the unmarked run as the premise that the plugin offers it at all.


182. **dependency-01 rows (T-02-84..87, M-2 and M-3).** T-02-84..86 follow T-02-42/43 and T-13-17: each class and its shared steps (`OptionalDependencyFixtures`) reference core, test-harness, JDK and batch-control types only. T-02-86 computes the omit list from the hpi manifests in `test-dependencies` (the required-dependency closure of cloudbees-folder, ionicons-api and (since R4-2) caffeine-api is kept, everything else is omitted), so a newly added test plugin is omitted automatically; the premise asserts that the five compile-time optional plugins and the four name-matched ones are in that list. The direct Build Now is sent with the crumb issuer turned off, because the child JVM of `RealJenkinsExtension` rejected the WebClient's crumb (the first run answered "No valid crumb", which would have made the refusal vacuous); the row asserts the refusal names approval and is not the crumb page. T-02-87 lists: `ReplayCause#getOriginalNumber/#isRebuilt`, `ReplayFlowFactoryAction`, `RestartDeclarativePipelineCause#getOriginRunNumber`, `RestartFlowFactoryAction#getOriginRunId` and field `originRunId`, `com.sonyericsson.rebuild.RebuildCause`, `CpsThread.current()`, `CpsFlowExecution#getOwner`, `FlowExecutionOwner#getExecutable/#getListener`, `NaginatorCause#getSourceBuildNumber`, `BuildRootAction`, `hudson.cli.BuildCommand$CLICause`, matrix-auth `AuthorizationContainer#getAllPermissionEntries` and `PermissionEntry#getType/#getSid`. Package names were taken from the upstream jars in `~/.m2`, not from src/main. It checks names only, not return types, because the report pins names. A mutation run (renaming `getOriginRunNumber` in the canary) failed with the name in the message. When the plugin gains or drops a name match, this list must change with it.

183. **D-59 move rows (T-SEC-53..62, hosting review 2026-10-02, MV-1..MV-3).** Moves are driven through the folders plugin's real endpoint, `POST <item url>move/move` with `destination`; the folders plugin (6.1106) matches the destination against `"/" + fullName`, so the rows send `/team`, `/parking`. `Items.move` is used only for the SYSTEM fixture in T-SEC-56. Native per-folder permissions come from matrix-auth's folder `AuthorizationMatrixProperty` (premise asserted with `hasPermission2`). Every refusal row has a twin in which the same move goes through (adding Delete on the source, or switching change control off), so a row cannot pass because moves are broken; T-SEC-57/58/60 put the refusal first as the guard. A refusal must be a 4xx: the folders plugin's own "destination not offered" answer is a silent 302 redirect without a move, which SPEC rules out ("answers with a plain message and is recorded"). The record must name the user and the moved item's full name before the move (`prod/x`) in its target or detail. Red at commit time (against the pre-D-59 HEAD): T-SEC-53..58/60/61 answer 302 and T-SEC-62's monitor stays quiet; T-SEC-59 is green by design (administrator twin).

184. **D-38a: a run request no longer needs Item/Build (T-05-07..13 and T-05-19 rewritten, T-05-21..24 added, T-02-09 given text updated).** Written from the rewritten SPEC item 5 line and D-38a/D-57. The former refusal rows become acceptance rows (nb without Build submits through the job form, the incident rerun and both service entries) and every one keeps a refused twin: without Request (`nr`, who holds Build so that Build cannot stand in for Request), without Item/Read (`nrd`), and for the web rerun without ViewHistory (`nv`, D-57). Refusal codes: the job form answers 4xx rather than strictly 403 because without Request the per-job action is absent (T-02-09: 404) and without Item/Read the job itself is invisible; the web rerun stays 403 (T-SEC-10). The ViewHistory requirement is pinned on the web rerun only: `IncidentService.rerun` is a Java entry that SPEC does not separately constrain, and the pre-D-38a code does not check it there, so adding it would widen D-38a. For `nrd` the service rerun may refuse with the job-not-found answer, since to that user the job does not exist. T-05-13 now pins that Request is checked on the requested job (formerly Build). The notice sentence is frozen verbatim. The REQUEST_CREATED notification is observed through the shipped e-mail notifier's plain-text body because `ops.Notification` exposes no free-text field a `BatchControlNotifier` could read (Request line in the slice report). Red at commit time (pre-D-38a HEAD): T-05-07/09/11/12/13/19/21/23 (403/404 or AccessDenied "missing the Job/Build permission"); T-05-08/10/22/24 green by design (guards).

185. **D-60 rows (T-06-89..96) and the context-path fix (T-06-59, T-UI-10/15/23 revised).** Written from SPEC item 6's D-60 line, D-60, P-03 and the coordinator's frozen contract: a requester's refused parameterized build answers 303 to `<job>/batch-control/` carrying `p.<NAME>=<value>`; `GET <job>/batch-control/?p.*` pre-fills only parameters the job defines of the kinds string, text, boolean and choice; password values never travel and are ignored if supplied; invalid choices and unknown names are ignored; values over 2000 characters are not carried; values are escaped; the POST never reads `p.*`; a user who may not request keeps the 400 refusal page inside `form.batch-control-refusal` with `X-Dialog-Title`. The submission goes through core's real parameters form (`build?delay=0sec`, JavaScript on, redirects off so that the 303 itself is asserted). Parameter controls are found through core's `<div name="parameter">` block (hidden `name` input, `value` control); core's password widget keeps its value in a hidden input. "Does not fall back to the classic build form" is asserted as the absence of a `parameters` form in the answer and in the redirect target; the new job page's dialog script itself is not driven by HtmlUnit (e2e). The raw sidebar href is now job-relative (`../../batch-control/grants/?...`, prefixed with the job URL by the sidebar), so T-UI-10/15 compare the href resolved against the page and dot-segment normalised (RFC 3986 5.2) with `<root URL>batch-control/grants/?...`; JenkinsRule serves under `/jenkins`, a non-root context path (premise asserted), and T-UI-15 adds a job two folders deep. `/job/X/buildWithParameters` from a browser is not covered: how a browser submission is told apart is not part of the contract. At commit time only T-06-93 is red (a 2001-character `p.P` is pre-filled by the GET).

186. **Configuration page polish (T-UI-27/28, hosting review 2026-10-02).** Screen-contract rows for the configuration under Manage Jenkins (`manage/batch-control-configuration/`). T-UI-27 forbids an Apply button by caption, `value` or `name` on any button or submit/button input of the page, with a Save button as premise; checked against the commit before ui-dev's polish (fc720fb), where it fails on core's `<button name="Apply" class="apply-button">`. T-UI-28 reads the "settings-subpage layout" as parity with the page itself (presence of `.jenkins-app-bar` and `#side-panel`) plus a breadcrumb under "Manage Jenkins", because the layout's markup is core's and not part of the contract; it already passed before the polish commit, so it is a regression guard. Both green at commit time.

187. **D-38a follow-up: DiscoverOnlyRequestScreenTest fixture and Request per folder (T-05-25).** The S-16 fixture filed b's requests against `secret-j` while b held Item/Build but no Item/Read there, which SPEC item 5 never allowed (D-38 and D-38a both require Item/Read); it passed only because the pre-D-38a code did not check Item/Read on the service path. The fixture now files those requests while b holds Item/Read on `secret-j` and restores the measured strategy (b discover-only) straight after, re-asserting the premise, so every row still measures a requester who can no longer read the job (the S-16 situation); no assertion of T-SEC-20..23 changed. The Build grants added for D-38 were dropped. setUp gains a guard: b's direct `RunRequestService.create` on `secret-j` is refused with AccessDeniedException and stores nothing (T-05-08/11 cover the same on other paths). T-05-25 pins the item scope of BatchControl/Request with a project-matrix folder grant. Green at commit time.

188. **T-UI-18 revised for D-60.** The renamed core build entry of a parameterised job opens core's parameters form; under SPEC item 6's D-60 line the requester's submission is no longer a refusal page but a 303 to the pre-filled Request Run form, so the row now asserts the 303, its Location (`batch-control/` with the typed `p.DATE`), and that the landing page names approval, carries the Request Run form with the value and shows no classic build form; the non-2xx refusal is kept for nobc, who may not request, and the blocking baseline for both. A suite-wide search found no other assertion of a requester's parameterised submission expecting non-2xx (QueueBlockTest T-06-02 asserts only blocking, anonymously; OwnerScenarioConfigChangeTest posts to an uncontrolled job; ApprovalNoticeTest was revised in note 185).

189. **Singular pager caption (T-UI-47, E2E-1 UX-9).** The request list's footer pattern in DiscoverOnlyRequestScreenTest now accepts `request` or `requests`, since the pager uses the singular for one item; T-SEC-20's assertions are otherwise unchanged (it lists three rows, so it still reads the plural).
