T-UI-47 adds the one-row case. A search for the former tab label "Change Records" found it only in a Javadoc sentence of GrantViolationDetailTest, no assertion; SectionTabsTest passes. Green at commit time.

190. **D-35e and D-35f (PR2, hosting review 2026-10-02).** *D-35e:* the legacy wrapper `BatchControlAuthorizationStrategy`, its load conversion and the S-04 unwrapping were removed, so the rows asserting them are withdrawn and their tests deleted: T-02-26 `t_02_26_legacyMatrixWrapperLoadsAsMatrixSubclass`, T-02-27 `t_02_27_legacyRoleWrapperLoadsAsRoleSubclass`, T-02-28 `t_02_28_legacyWrapperAroundOtherStrategyIsUnwrapped`, T-02-29 `t_02_29_nestedLegacyWrapperLoadsAsSingleSubclass`, T-02-36 `t_02_36_legacyGlobalMatrixWrapperIsUnwrappedNotConverted` (the whole `StrategyUpgradeTest`), with the fixture `LegacyWrapperXml` and `StrategyFixtures.LEGACY_WRAPPER`. The test count drops by five on purpose. The per-item warning for converting a global matrix stays covered by the migration rows. T-02-42/43 keep their absence-boot purpose: they now install the Batch Control strategy directly and still check a reload and a restart. A config.xml naming the removed class is not tested: per the owner (D-35e) core then shows its boot-failure page, which needs no plugin behaviour and is not cheap to boot in a test. *D-35f:* T-02-88..95 cover every role-strategy 918 save path named by D-35f, each with a plain-strategy guard; T-02-96..98 are the descriptor surface guard (scanner + resolution, live URLs, positive guard) and are meant to fail when a future role-strategy release calls a descriptor member the Batch Control descriptor lacks; T-02-99 renders the role-strategy pages (on 918 Assign Roles is the RoleStrategyConfig index, not `assign-roles`). T-02-17 is rewritten: the plain class now comes from the security page, and the monitor text must not name Manage Roles.

191. **PR6 tab bar rows (T-UI-40..46, hosting review 2026-10-02).** Markup frozen by ui-dev: `nav[data-batch-control-tabs]` with `a[data-batch-control-tab=<section>]` (sections overview, requests, activations, grants, history, changes, dashboard, incidents) and a badge element whose class contains `badge` inside a tab. "May access" is measured as "the section URL answers 200 to the user", so the rows compare the tab bar and the context menu with real reachability instead of restating the permission rules; the only rule asserted directly is grants only while change control is on. T-UI-46 needs a real Jenkins: on the flat class path of a plain JenkinsRule every page rendered, while in a real Jenkins every Batch Control page answered 500 before 4d11920. The test was run against 01ab309 and failed there with "admin GET /batch-control/ must answer 200".

192. **Browser page for a refused move (T-SEC-63).** D-59 asks for a D-48-style answer, and D-48 says a browser form save shows the text on a page with the standard layout; T-SEC-53..56 keep covering the plain answer. The hostile name uses only characters Jenkins' item-name check accepts (quotes, apostrophes, `=`, parentheses), since `<`, `>` and `&` cannot occur in an item name. The row was run against 3637153 (before the browser page) and failed there with "a browser gets an HTML page, got text/plain".

193. **D-35f(a) sid validation rows (T-02-100..107).** The Batch Control role strategy descriptor answers `checkSidName` (role-strategy PR #766) and `checkName` (918) itself. A private security realm with real accounts is used because the test harness dummy realm accepts every name, so "unknown" could not be checked. T-02-107 needs its own JVM: role-strategy enables its ItemRoles/AgentRoles permissions only through a system property read once when the class loads. Run against 122bafe (before the descriptor answered `checkSidName`), 6 of the 7 rows in RoleSidValidationTest failed; T-02-106 passed there as intended, because it pins the unchanged 918 `checkName` behaviour.

194. **Request Change Permission link with one parameter (T-UI-10/15, e2e-06 DEF-01).** The new job page's menu escaped the `&` of `?scopeType=JOB&scopeFullName=...`, so the form lost the job. The link now carries `scopeFullName` only and the grant form defaults to the JOB scope. T-UI-10/15 compare the resolved href with `<root URL>batch-control/grants/?scopeFullName=<encoded full name>` and, because the defect was visible as a form that was not about the job, follow the link and assert the form's scope type is JOB, the scope field holds the full name, the page says "Prefilled for" the job and CONFIGURE is checked. Green at commit time.

195. **D-38b rows (T-05-26..30; T-05-25 follows the redirect).** Written from the SPEC item 2 D-38b line, D-38b and spec-review-S5 B-1. The actor `fr` now holds Item/Read and Request only through the folder property of `ops` (Overall/Read globally), so jobs outside the folder are invisible to fr and "requests of jobs they cannot see" is measurable. Submissions in these rows follow redirects with their own client, because `ApproverFormFixtures.post` turns redirects off (`client`, line 60) and that is how T-05-25 missed the 404 on the landing page. The root and sections are asserted reachable for fr after fr has a request of their own, which satisfies both the SPEC line and D-38b's "holds Request globally or has requests of their own"; reachability before any own request is not asserted. T-05-30 is the 404 guard for a user without Batch Control permission and without own requests. Red against 96e2a24 (before D-38b): T-05-25/26 land on a 404, T-05-27 404 on `changeApprover`, T-05-28 404 at the root, T-05-29 403 on the ACTIVATE request; T-05-30 green (guard).

196. **APPROVERS_CHANGED notice (T-05-31/32, spec-review-S5 m-3).** The re-designated approver decides with the APPROVERS_CHANGED mail, so it carries the D-38a notice under the same condition as REQUEST_CREATED (T-05-23/24); observed through the shipped e-mail notifier as in note 184. Green against 96e2a24.

197. **Redirect query cap (T-06-97, security-33 S-33-03).** The D-60 redirect query is capped at 4000 URL-encoded characters (counting `?`, `&`, `p.<name>=` and the value), filled in parameter-definition order; a value that would pass the cap is skipped and a later shorter one may still fit; the 2000-character per-value bound (T-06-93) is unchanged. Values of plain ASCII letters are used so that encoding does not change lengths: `?p.A=` + 1500 = 1505, `&p.B=` + 1500 brings 3010, C would bring 4515 and is skipped, `&p.D=short-d` brings 3022. Green at commit time (ui-dev 3a5baf7).

198. **Active-grant URLs in a real Jenkins (T-UI-48).** The GET-only redirect handlers of the active-grants section were replaced by views (reviewer comment 2026-09-27, ui-dev aadb8da). The row runs under `RealJenkinsExtension`, as T-UI-46 does, because a plain JenkinsRule's flat class path has hidden view-resolution failures before; it asserts the 302 and its resolved Location under the non-root context path for an administrator and a RequestGrant holder, 405 (not 404) for a GET of `revoke`, and a refusal for a user without a grants permission. Green at commit time.

199. **D-38b service rows (T-05-33..35).** Written from the SPEC item 2 D-38b line and D-38b; the service entry names were given by the coordinator (core-dev 68d9c01) and the signatures read from the compiled public API only (`javap -public`), not from src/main: `cancel(String)`, `changeApprovers(String, List)`, `canCancel(request)`, `canChangeApprovers(request)` (answering for the current authentication) and `hasOwnRequests(Authentication)` on RunRequestService and ActivationService. Losing Request is modelled by replacing the folder property with one that keeps Item/Read, so the refusal measures Request alone. Green at commit time (the methods did not exist before 68d9c01).

200. **D-59a moved-job lock and the refusal page link (T-SEC-64..69).** In every row u1's move is allowed by D-59 (native Delete on the source, native Create on the destination), so the rows test only the D-59a lock. The HELD record must name the job and contain "mov". T-SEC-64 has no negative twin: a user who cannot read the item gets 404 from its move endpoint before any refusal page exists. With the test files copied onto the committed HEAD 84c7c9a (core-dev's D-59a work still uncommitted), T-SEC-65/66 failed and the rest passed; with core-dev's work in progress, all six pass.

201. **Global matrix per-item warning and the matrix variant's name (T-02-108..110).** SPEC 8 says the warning comes "before its install action". Core's administrative-monitor markup puts the controls container first in the DOM and shows it beside the message, so DOM order cannot express that. The rows therefore assert that the warning is in the message text shown with the button, and that the confirmation question repeats it. A first version asserted DOM order and failed on correct markup; it was the assertion that was wrong, not the product.

202. **UI polish markup evidence (T-UI-50..52).** These rows give automated evidence for checklist items that only had e2e coverage. A control is found by its endpoint (`href`, `data-url`, `formaction`, or the submit button of a form whose action ends in `/cancel`, `/revoke` or `/revert`), never by its caption. The Revert control is looked for on `manage/batch-control-configuration/` and then on `manage/configure`. All three rows failed on fc720fb, the commit before the polish.

203. **Request Run in the new job page's app bar (T-UI-55).** Automated evidence for a checklist item that only had e2e coverage. It uses core's public menu API (`Action#getGroup`, `Action#getSemantic`), so it checks the declared placement and style rather than the rendered page.

204. **Rebuild Last in the job context menu (T-06-98/99, e2e-07 DEF-06).** The flag is set as a system property and cleared after each test. T-06-98 failed on 49472ed, before the fix, with "'Rebuild Last' has a null URL" in the `event` of the new menu model, so the row reproduces the defect. The job-without-a-completed-build case is rebuild's own defect (LIMITATIONS 45) and is not asserted.

210. **Discover-only move destination (T-SEC-70/71, backlog #74).** Written from SPEC 8 (D-59), issue #74 and security-33 S-33-05. The "folders plugin's normal answer" is measured, not assumed: it is the answer to a destination that does not exist (`/no-such-folder`), and the Discover-only destination must get the same status. The 403 S-33-05 describes could not be reproduced from outside: with native Delete, with granted Delete, with Create on `secret` without Read, with global Create and with a CREATE window on `team`, the folders plugin answered 302 for `/secret` on 39f4bad (before the fix) as well as after 02ca78a. Both rows are green on both commits; T-SEC-70 is a regression bound, not a reproduction.

211. **Requester Build notice across views (T-05-36..39, backlog #75).** Written from SPEC 5 (D-38a), issue #75 and the behaviour core-dev reported (per-request cache, 5 minutes on the plugin clock, dropped when the request is saved). A test `AbstractPasswordBasedSecurityRealm` counts `loadUserByUsername2` per name; only lookups of the requester are counted, after REQUEST_CREATED reached the notifiers. On 39f4bad (no plugin cache) 5 views already cost one lookup of nb, so T-05-36 does not discriminate the fix; it stays as an upper bound. T-05-37..39 assert correctness only (the cache must never hide a change of Build after a new request, a save or the cache life); they pass on both commits. The stale answer inside the cache life is deliberately not asserted.

212. **Activations section for per-folder Request holders (T-05-40, backlog #87).** Written from SPEC 2 (D-38b line, now naming D-38c) and DECISIONS D-38c. fr is admitted to the root by an own run request only, so the row measures that the activations section no longer needs an own activation request. Red on 39f4bad (403), green on the backlog/ux head.

213. **Grant request landing (T-08-65, backlog #89).** Same landing contract as T-05-26 for run requests; the submission follows redirects with its own client. The table width part of #89 (1280 px) is a visual property and is left to e2e. Red on 39f4bad (landed on the Grants list).

214. **Revocation reason of the kill switch (T-08-66, backlog #85, D-63).** On 39f4bad the record detail already said "change control was switched off", so the record half failed there only on D-63's wording; the grants screen half is the real gap (it read like an individual revoke). The screen is opened after change control is turned back on (with the setter, which revokes nothing), because the reason must survive on the listed closed window.

215. **Windows behind a move (T-09-24, backlog #84).** Asserted structurally: each window id must appear in the record's grantId or detail, and grantId must be the CREATE window (core-dev's reported contract); the exact sentence is not asserted. The refusal page's "Grant" column (UX-4) is not covered. Red on 39f4bad (only the CREATE id).

216. **Pre-filled grant form on the move refusal (T-SEC-72, backlog #83).** Links are found in `#main-panel` by target (`batch-control/grants` with `scopeFullName=`), never by caption. Only Delete is missing in the fixture, so a link for `team` would offer a window that cannot help; the row refuses it. The Move page hint of #83 (UX-3) is not covered. Red on 39f4bad (no such link).

217. **Overview counts (T-UI-56, backlog #88).** The count is read as the first stand-alone number in the body link to the section or its three nearest ancestors, so the row does not depend on captions. The shipped overview counts requests, activations and grants; incidents are not counted and the row does not demand it. Red on 39f4bad (no section links in the body).

218. **Naming strategy on moves (T-SEC-73/74, D-59b).** Written from SPEC 8 (D-59 line as amended) and D-59b. The Create item role uses `team(/team-.*)?` so that Create on the container `team` is a measured premise, and the naming strategy refuses `team/x` while it accepts `team/team-ok`. "Naming the naming rule" is asserted as the word "naming" in the record's target or detail. T-SEC-73 fails on 6ba5aec (302, the item moved) and passes with core-dev's uncommitted MoveGuard change.

219. **Change control off (T-SEC-75).** The twin refusal comes first so that the row cannot pass because moves are never refused. It fails on 6ba5aec at the twin, for the same reason as T-SEC-73.

220. **Job-level URLs after StaplerProxy removal (T-UI-70..72, #71, D-64).** Real-class-loader smoke (`RealJenkinsExtension`, as T-UI-46/48). D-64 moves the activation form to its own action at `<item>/batch-control-activation/` (submit `.../submit`, POST) for jobs and computed folders and drops `<item>/batch-control/activation` without a redirect; the run-request and computed-folder activation actions lose `StaplerProxy`. The rows assert: `<job>/batch-control/` with the D-60 pre-fill unchanged; the new form and submission answer an allowed user (GET followed through redirects, POST not followed); every activation link on the job page answers 200; the old URL answers 404 to the allowed user on GET and POST (redirects not followed, so a 3xx also fails) and stores nothing; 404 for a user without Request and for one who cannot read the item. The crumb is fetched over HTTP as the logged-in user, because `createCrumbedUrl` computes it on the test thread and a real Jenkins rejects it (403 "No valid crumb"). The first version (old URLs, before D-64) was green against 39f4bad. The existing rows that used the old URL now use the new one, with no assertion changed otherwise: ActivationFixtures (`submitActivation`), ActivationRequestTest T-06a-16/21/22, ActivationBreadcrumbTest T-UI-22/24, ActivationScreenTest T-06a-38 and its helper (the job page's link must contain `batch-control-activation`), plus Javadoc in ActivationComputedFolderTest.

221. **One source for pending counts (T-UI-73..77, #76, D-61).** API from the coordinator and `javap -public` on core-dev 5f40f4d: `countPendingFor(Authentication)` on RunRequestService, GrantRequestService and ActivationService returning `model.PendingCount` (`getAwaitingDecision`, `getOwn`, `getCount`, `isEmpty`, `NONE`), plus `ActivationService#listAwaitingDecision(Authentication)`. Rows use users who are only requester or only approver, so how `getCount()` combines both numbers is not pinned; `isDecisions()` is not asserted. "The badge shows the same number" is asserted per tab (requests, grants, activations) with the T-UI-44 badge reader: badge text = `getCount()`, no badge for 0. Losing Approve is modelled by replacing the strategy after designation (designation itself may require Approve). Green against 5f40f4d with ui-dev's uncommitted SectionTabs/ActivationsSection change in the working tree.

222. **Ids.** T-UI-70..77 and T-02-120 are taken out of sequence so that parallel backlog worktrees, which add rows from T-UI-56 and T-02-111 upward, do not collide.

223. **role-strategy minimum in the manifest (T-02-120/121, #78).** What is proven: the manifest Jenkins reads for the plugin under test declares role-strategy as an optional dependency with a minimum of 918 or newer, and the running Jenkins parsed it that way. The test manifest `the.hpl` is used because surefire runs before `package`, so `target/*.hpi` does not exist yet; maven-hpi-plugin builds both manifests from the same POM data, but the `.hpi` manifest itself is not read. The minimum is compared rather than pinned to one string because the scheduled role-strategy-latest build (#77) raises `role-strategy.version`. D-61 is now cited by the tab bar, badge and context-menu rows T-UI-40..46 (doc only); T-UI-47 (pager caption) is not a D-61 row and is left as it was.

224. **Jenkins refuses Batch Control next to role-strategy 898 (T-02-122, #78).** Proven locally with the 898 hpi copied by hand to `target/test-classes/old-plugins/role-strategy.hpi`: Jenkins boots, role-strategy 898 is active, and batch-control is listed under the plugin manager's failed plugins (not under `getPlugins()`) with "Update required: Role-based Authorization Strategy (role-strategy 898.vc050ed2424ca_) to be updated to 918.v91e5468d8db_2 or higher". Without that file the row is skipped by an assumption, never passed; it becomes a CI check only once the build copies `org.jenkins-ci.plugins:role-strategy:898.vc050ed2424ca_:hpi` to that path (request to release-manager). The class references no Batch Control or role-strategy type, because its step runs in a Jenkins where Batch Control is not loaded.

230. **D-69 (no load-time migration).** T-03-21 (MultiApproverLegacyLoadTest, the single-`approver` to set conversion) is withdrawn and its test removed; T-03-07 no longer asserts the compatibility `getApprover()` value, which SPEC 3 no longer names. Other tests still call `RunRequest#getApprover()` on requests created by the current plugin (RunRequestServiceTest, StoreDurabilityTest, OwnerScenario*); they do not test a conversion and are unchanged.

231. **UUID identifiers (T-08-90..95, D-68).** "Canonical" is asserted as `UUID.fromString(id).toString().equals(id)` (36 characters, lower case); the UUID version is not pinned. Earlier-format records are made from records the current plugin wrote: every UUID is replaced by an id of the form `yyyyMMdd-HHmmss-<6 chars>` (ARCHITECTURE 5) in the content and names of all files under `batch-control/` between two sessions, so references between files stay consistent and the startup index sees only the earlier ids. Each restart row asserts the replaced UUID no longer resolves, so a 200 comes from the earlier-format record. The window URL `grants/active/<id>/` is followed through redirects; an unknown window id must answer >= 400.

232. **R4-4 (notice cache keyed by request id).** T-05-36..39 are unchanged; T-05-90 adds the reverse direction (losing Item/Build), which a cache shared between requests or not dropped on save would hide.

233. **FOLDER_ONLY scope (T-08-100..108, D-65).** Written from SPEC 8 and D-65 with the owner's rulings: CREATE only directly in the folder, DELETE only on direct non-folder items (not on a nested folder, a multibranch project in the folder or the folder itself; a move of a nested folder out is therefore refused, T-08-109..111), CONFIGURE of a nested folder itself allowed (it is an item whose parent is the folder). Configure/Delete are asserted both as the item's ACL answer and, for jobs, as the HTTP result (config.xml, `doDelete`). Each FOLDER_ONLY row has a FOLDER guard so it cannot pass because windows never confer. The grants form's FOLDER_ONLY option (rendering) is not asserted; T-08-105 asserts the POST binding.

234. **T-04-08/T-04-10 under D-68.** The rows required request ids in the timestamp form carrying the plugin clock's time. D-68 replaced that for requests and windows, so they now assert a canonical UUID (T-04-08: under each non-ASCII-digit locale; T-04-10: plus `createdAt` equal to the plugin clock's instant, the time a request is ordered by). Change record ids keep the timestamp form and their assertions are unchanged. No other row or test asserted the conversions D-69 removed (single `from`/`to` of an approver change, inline diff of a change record, grants without `grantRequestId`).

235. **caffeine-api is required (R4-2).** The requester-build cache uses caffeine-api as a required plugin dependency, so T-02-86's kept set (`OptionalDependencyFixtures.REQUIRED`) names it; without it batch-control itself cannot load. The row still omits every optional plugin.

240. **Overview counts and dashboard bound (T-UI-90..92, D-67).** D-67 removed the #88 count table and the pending banner, so T-UI-56 (which demanded the table) is withdrawn rather than loosened and its test method replaced by T-UI-90. T-UI-90 uses distinctive counts (7, 5, 4, total 16) so the banner check cannot match unrelated text, and it keeps the badges as the positive guard. The dashboard rows count a run as shown when its job name is in the visible body text (tab bar, select, datalist, script excluded), so a job filter listing every job does not count. "Links to History for the rest" is read as: the left-out runs are reachable from the dashboard's History link, History's own paging included. Red on bec34ba (banner text "7 run requests are awaiting your decision", no History link from the dashboard).

241. **model-link (T-UI-93/94, R4-11).** A job/run link is a body anchor whose path is exactly the job or the build. On bec34ba the dashboard's run link had no class (red), and the request list offered no job link at all, so the list is checked only for the links it does offer (SPEC does not pin the list's columns, note 27).

245. **List layout (T-UI-95/96, D-66).** Rows are found by the id in their link and ordered by document order of `#main-panel` links outside the tab bar, so neither section captions nor markup are pinned. The active window may link by its request id or its window id. "Not the first thing" is read as: a grant request form, if still on the page, comes after the pending row. Red on bec34ba (the form preceded the lists).

246. **Revoke from the detail page (T-UI-97, D-66, R4-12).** The control is found by its endpoint (a path ending in `/revoke`, in href, action, formaction or a data attribute) and posted with a crumb. The permission rule is the list's (Manage; GrantWebTest). The confirmation before revoking is browser behaviour (a dialog) and is left to e2e. Red on bec34ba (the detail page offered no revoke).

247. **Request dialogs (T-UI-98..100, D-66, R4-9, R4-14).** HtmlUnit runs without JavaScript, so the rows test the server side of the dialogs: an entry is any element outside the tab bar whose href or data attribute names a same-instance URL, and the page offers an entry when that URL renders the request form posting to the existing endpoint. A submission must redirect to the new request's detail page. That the dialog opens on the current page and follows the redirect is left to e2e. On bec34ba T-UI-98 was red (no entry on the grants page and no folder entry); T-UI-99 and T-UI-100 were already green (the Request Run link and the redirects existed), and stay as guards for D-66 and D-60.

248. **Existing rows adapted to D-66.** D-66 moved the grant request form off the grants list page to `grants/new` (the content of a dialog); ui-dev named the public surface, so the rows now pin it. T-UI-10/15: the entry resolves to `grants/new?scopeFullName=<encoded name>`; context path, encoding and the prefilled form after following it are unchanged. T-UI-13/14: the prefill and the no-reflection checks run on `grants/new`; the blank-form guard opens `grants/new` without a query. T-UI-26: the grants page carries no request form and offers `[data-batch-control-request=grant]`; on `grants/new` a list table is no longer a premise, and if one is there the form and the "240" refusal still precede it. T-08-49: the re-request entry may be a dialog button (target from a data-* URL); the expired row is matched on normalized text, because the new cells have no whitespace between them and the raw text glued "Expired" to its neighbours. None of these drops a behaviour D-66 kept.

249. **D-66 public surface (T-UI-101..105, ui-dev dac5094).** Lists `table[data-batch-control-list=pending|active|ended]` with the paging parameters `pendingPage/activePage/endedPage`, the activations marker `small[data-batch-control-awaiting]`, the fragments `job/<j>/batch-control/dialog` and `grants/dialog`, `dialog=true` submissions (400 re-render, redirect on success), the redirect of the former `grants/?scopeFullName=` link, and `POST grants/<id>/revoke` (T-UI-97). An approved activation may be listed as active rather than ended, so T-UI-96 accepts either for it. SPEC does not say whether an approver who is not designated sees a pending activation, so T-UI-103 only requires that a2 has no marked row. Red on bec34ba: T-UI-95..98, 101..104 and the adapted T-UI-10/13/14/15/26; T-UI-99, 100 and 105 were already green there and are guards.

250. **Filter forms keep the crumb out of the URL (T-UI-106, e2e-12 DEF-07).** No SPEC acceptance line covers the URL of a GET filter form, hence a T-UI row; the crumb in a URL leaks into history, bookmarks, logs and the Referer. The defect comes from core's `hudson-behavior.js`, so the web client runs with JavaScript on (a client without JavaScript could not reproduce it). Forms are found by their submit caption ("Filter", "Show"); the Changes/Incidents month field is found by `type=month` or a name containing "month", and its name is read from the page, not pinned. Each page also has to navigate and keep the value, so a form that drops the filter cannot pass.

## red-team 시나리오 제외 사유 (red-team-01, 매트릭스 행 미추가)

| RT | 제외 사유 |
|---|---|
| RT-04 (FOLDER prefix 오판) | 기존 행과 중복: T-SEC-03(unit, `team/batch` vs `team/batch-other` 경계 판정)과 T-08-11(integration, 폴더 안/밖 통합 검증)이 동일 공격을 커버. POC C에서도 검증됨. 새 엣지 없음. |
| RT-08 (다계정 자가 결재) | 자동 테스트 불가(설계 범위 밖). 플러그인은 계정↔실인 매핑을 알 수 없다 — 조직 통제(IdP 계정 위생) 영역. 비고 12의 README 문서화 요청으로 대체. |
| RT-09 (권한 상실 결재자) | 기존 행과 중복: T-03-03이 "목록 등재 + Approve 권한 상실 → 결재 거부"를 그대로 커버. 부수 위험(요청 PENDING 고착)은 T-07-01(pendingTimeout 만료)로 회수 검증됨. 지정 시점 차단은 SPEC이 요구하지 않음(결재 시점 검사만 명시). |
| RT-12 (잡 이름 경로 탈출·초장문) | 기존 행 확장으로 흡수: 경로 탈출(`../`)은 T-SEC-04가 이미 커버, 시나리오가 추가한 실질 엣지(제어문자·255자 초과 이름)를 T-SEC-04의 Given/Then에 병합했다. 별도 행 불필요. |
| security-08 S-07 (computed-folder children not name-restricted) | Not a defect to test: D-40a and SPEC item 8 keep indexing-created children outside the restriction and send it to LIMITATIONS. |
| security-08 S-13 (CLI name outliving its command on a pooled thread) | Not reproducible from outside: it needs `handleException` itself to throw, which no public CLI input causes. The observable guarantee (a script creation is UNKNOWN and gets nothing from a restricted grant) is already in the D-40 rows. |

## 요약 (Phase 2 최종 — red-team 병합 후)

- **총 행 수: 345** (+ the security-08 regression rows T-SEC-35..51, note 60; + the GrantRequest `changeApprover` rows T-03-22..26, note 59; + the D-36/D-37/D-40 rows T-03-07..21, T-08-37..46 and T-13-01..19, note 58; one retired row, T-SEC-13, still counted; + the plugin-interaction and no-op-save rows T-06-20..42 and T-09-12..18 (#20, #34, #36), notes 54, 55 and 56; + the security-07 regression rows T-SEC-32..34, note 57; SPEC 도출 119 + T-RT 16 + Phase 4 security regressions T-SEC-08..14 + coverage-gap row T-SEC-15 + security-02 regressions T-SEC-16..18 + owner scenario rows T-OS-01..10, note 32 + D-30 marker-re-use audit rows T-06-17/18/19, notes 15 and 37 + D-31 new-job default rows T-08-19..29, notes 38 and 39 + ui-dev screen-contract rows T-UI-01..06, notes 40 and 43 + the P-03 recording-path row T-SEC-19, note 44 + D-34 creation-vs-activation rows T-08-30..36, note 46 + the issue-12 UI-lane regression rows T-SEC-20..26 (S-16) and T-SEC-27..31 (S-22), notes 47 and 49 + the issue-12 UI-lane screen-contract rows T-UI-07..21 (N-01, U-01, U-02, U-07), note 48 + the hosting-review rows T-02-06..09 (D-41, #31), note 50 + the D-35a/b/c/d strategy rows T-02-10..43 (#30, security-05), notes 51, 52 and 53) — counts below are measured from the table above, not carried forward
- **우선순위**: P0 224 / P1 113 / P2 8 (T-SEC-35..38/40/44..49 are P0 (name-restriction bypass, stored parameters, decision authority), T-SEC-39/41/42/43/50/51 P1, note 60; T-03-22..26 are P0 — a requester-driven designation change on an active grant window, a 403 permission-boundary check and a 405 GET-refusal, note 59; note 58 rows: P0 24 - decision authority, the name restriction, the notifier-failure and no-mail guarantees - and P1 20; of the #20/#34/#36 rows T-06-20 (the Request Run entry, a usability row), the false-positive guards T-06-21/26/32/35 and T-09-17/18 are P1, the rest P0 — run blocking and the one-record guarantee; T-02-10/11/12/16/22/24/25/26/32/34/35/36/38/39/42/43 are P0 — per-item authorization, grant expiry, the self-grant guards, the legacy matrix load and the migrate/revert 403 row, note 51; T-08-30..36 are P0 — D-34 closes the hole through which a job created inside a change window kept running after the window expired, and T-08-26/27 are the release path that keeps generated jobs from being permanently dead; T-08-19..29 are P0 — D-31 closes a hole through which any new job ran uncontrolled, and its automation safety net T-08-26..29 guards against stopping unattended CI; T-SEC-16/17/18 are P0 — they pin whether a permission can be conferred; T-SEC-20..26 are P0 — S-16 made the request screens answer HTTP 200 with an empty list, so a caller could not see their own request and nothing said why; T-SEC-27..31 are P0 — S-22 is a formula-injection sink reachable from a decision comment and from a job name with a leading space; T-UI-07..21 are P0 except the negative twins T-UI-08/17, which are P1; T-SEC-14 moved P2→P1, see note 29; T-08-14..18 are P0 — the change window's read paths are the primary human path and were observed broken against a real Jenkins in Phase 5, see note 31; of the owner rows T-OS-01..06/09/10 are P0 — rejection, approval traceability, live control toggles and mid-run configuration changes all carry operational consequences — and T-OS-07/08 are P1 control groups; T-06-17/18/19 are P0 — D-30 makes the visibility of a blocked security control an acceptance criterion, and the 403 row follows the item 12 gate; T-SEC-32 is P0 — it is a run-blocking/queue row (coordinator rule); T-SEC-33/34 are P1 — security-07 rates S-02 MEDIUM and S-04 LOW, note 57)
- **Lane A storage rows (notes 62-69, 80-84, #13/#17/#18/#25, security-10)**: +21 rows (T-04-16/17, T-10-10, T-12-09/10 added with notes 80-84; T-04-16 P0, the rest P1) - T-04-05..15, T-10-08/09, T-12-06..08; P0 4 (T-04-05/06/12/15), P1 12; unit 2 (T-04-05/11), integration 14. Not yet folded into the totals above (a parallel lane edits them). T-04-15 (note 69) is a regression row for a real defect found and fixed in commit 08f342c, added after the rest of this lane.
- **계층**: unit 8 / integration 328 / e2e 9
- **ID 그룹별 분포**: T-01 6, T-02 43, T-03 26, T-04 4, T-05 6, T-06 42, T-07 7, T-08 46, T-09 18, T-10 7, T-11 7, T-12 5, T-13 19, T-CFG 3, T-SEC 51, T-E2E 8, T-RT 16, T-OS 10, T-UI 21
- **T-RT 우선순위**: P0 9 (T-RT-01/02/03/05/06/14/15/16/17), P1 5 (T-RT-07/10/11/13/19), P2 2 (T-RT-18/20)
- **red-team 제안 채택 확정(비고 8, D-16~D-23 반영)**: R-1→T-RT-01, R-2→T-RT-05, R-3→T-RT-10(준용)·T-RT-11, R-4→T-RT-13, R-5→T-RT-14/15/16/17, R-6→T-RT-03, R-7→T-RT-18/19/20, R-8→T-RT-02/17
- **Owner scenario coverage map (note 32)**: S-1 rejection → new T-OS-01/02/03 (existing T-05-04 covers only the empty-comment refusal); S-2 approval↔run traceability → new T-OS-04, positive links already in T-05-05 and T-10-04; S-3 turning a control on mid-run → new T-OS-05/06 (nothing covered in-flight behaviour before); S-4 uncontrolled control group → new T-OS-07/08, with the marker single-use contract already in T-RT-02 and the standing-approval model difference recorded in note 32; S-5 configuration change mid-run → new T-OS-09 (T-09-01/02 cover recording on an idle job only); S-6 approver hand-over → already covered by T-03-04/T-03-05/T-RT-07, new T-OS-10 adds the never-designated approver boundary, delegation lines out of scope per note 33
- **Owner scenario 우선순위**: P0 8 (T-OS-01/02/03/04/05/06/09/10), P1 2 (T-OS-07/08)
- **D-30 coverage (notes 15, 37)**: the previously undefined record location for a blocked marker re-use is now covered by T-06-17 (independent audit record: dedicated type, requestId + job, actor, timestamp; exported in changes.csv; findable on the dashboard by actor), T-06-18 (a clean approved run leaves no such record — the falsifiability guard) and T-06-19 (no exposure without ViewHistory). T-RT-02 keeps the blocking assertion and now carries a footnote pointing at these rows; the P-04 proposal is settled by D-30
- **D-31 coverage (notes 38, 39)**: the new-job default is covered by T-08-19..24 (the default applies whoever creates the job and through whichever path — New Item form, REST `createItem`, CLI `create-job`, copy, Job DSL seed, and a plain user inside a grant window, with T-08-24 asserting the administrator and the user outcomes are equal), T-08-25 (run control off forces nothing — the falsifiability guard) and the automation safety net T-08-26..29 (timer, upstream and SCM causes still build the new job; only the human paths are refused). T-RT-05 keeps the D-17 grant-window case and now carries a footnote saying D-31 widened its scope. Two D-31 questions SPEC leaves open are deliberately unasserted and recorded in note 39: an explicit `approvalRequired=false` supplied at creation time, and retroactivity for jobs that already existed.
- **D-34 coverage (note 46)**: creating a job no longer puts it into service. The three-switch default is covered by T-08-30 (New Item form), T-08-31 (REST `createItem` with a cron — and the refused firing, the behavioural core), T-08-32 (CLI `create-job`), T-08-33 (the upstream door), T-08-35 (a copy of a live cron job) and T-08-36 (a Job DSL seed run), with T-08-34 as the falsifiability guard (run control off locks nothing). The release path is T-08-26/27 (rewritten: they used to assert that a *newly created* job builds from a timer/upstream, the contract D-34 reverses), the width of the lock is bounded by T-08-28 (the SCM cause still passes) and its intended effect by T-08-29 (the human path stays refused). T-RT-05's cron assertion is restored — its original R-2 demand was exactly D-34 — and D-32's computed-child exclusion survives unchanged in T-10-07. Five questions SPEC leaves open (an explicit switch value in the creation payload, the unlock being gated only while change control is on, copies, the silence of an unattended refusal, and approving the activation itself) are deliberately unasserted and recorded in note 46.
- **Screen-contract coverage (note 40)**: the approval screen's recent-run window (ui-dev, commit `d6c0878`) is covered by T-UI-01 (the 5/10/20/50 allow-list and the default of 5), T-UI-02 (**the value is trimmed before the allow-list is consulted**, so a padded allowed value such as `runs=10 ` is honoured as 10 and only a value that is still off the list after trimming falls back to 5, always silently and with no error page — owner ruling 2026-09-26: trimming a hand-typed query parameter is ordinary web input handling, so `10 ` is an allowed value and not an unsupported one; T-UI-02 initially asserted the opposite and was the assertion that was too wide, not a product defect), T-UI-03 and T-UI-04 (both P0 — no `runs` value discloses a run of a job the viewer may not read or of a job that has been deleted, so the window cannot be used around P-09) T-UI-05 (the post-approval notice is bound to `APPROVED` + `executedRunId == null`, measured through the `batch-control-approved-notice` id ui-dev keeps as a test hook, so the row asserts the notice itself and not merely the state it hangs off) and T-UI-06 (note 43). These rows freeze an implementation-defined screen contract rather than a SPEC acceptance criterion; whether it should become one is left to a human, and the rows are dropped with the feature if the answer is no.
- **P-03 and D-33 coverage (notes 44, 45 — the last two open P0/P1 rows of Phase 5)**: T-SEC-07 was the only empty P0 row in the matrix ("pending P-03 decision"); with the owner's ruling (keep masked storage, accept that an approved run cannot reproduce the secret) it now pins the guarantee **and** the limitation through the browser request form, with a non-sensitive parameter of the same job as the false-positive guard on every surface (request record, detail screen, store files, all four CSV exports, dashboard, launched build), and T-SEC-19 pins the same guarantee on the recording path that never goes through the request screen. T-E2E-06 was failing e2e because it demanded a notice inside a screen D-33 forbids the plugin from touching; it now asserts that the denial screen is core's own and that the expiry guidance plus re-request link live on `/batch-control/grants/`, staying at the e2e layer for the reasons in note 45 (its automatable core is already T-08-17/18).
- **issue-12 UI-lane coverage (notes 47, 48, 49)**: the four screen findings fixed on `fix/issue-12-ui` are pinned as follows. **S-16** (Item/Discover emptied the request screens): T-SEC-20 the populated list with a numeric footer equal to its row count, T-SEC-22 the explicit "job is not available" branch, T-SEC-23 an `hudson.ExpressionFactory2` log with no `AccessDeniedException` across the three page loads, T-SEC-24/25 the helper and the visibility predicate (null, never a throw), T-SEC-26 the incident screen including the rerun approver list, with T-SEC-21 as the labelled control row. **S-22** (CSV formula cells led by whitespace): T-SEC-27/28/29 on the encoder, plus the two end-to-end cells an attacker controls - T-SEC-30 the approver's decision comment and T-SEC-31 a job name beginning with a space, which was found during the fix and is not in the security report; the existing D-18 row T-RT-11 is unchanged. **N-01** (an out-of-range parameter value answered 500): T-UI-07 with T-UI-08 keeping the three outcomes that were already correct. **U-01** (no way to request change permission from a job): T-UI-09..15, one row per ANDed condition plus the prefill, the XSS non-reflection and the folder path. **U-02** (two entries both reading "Request Run"): T-UI-16/17/18. **U-07** (diff size and time vocabulary): T-UI-19 on the screen, T-UI-20/21 as unit rows. Which of these fail against the pre-fix code, and which are controls that do not, is recorded row by row in note 47.
